# Hardening

Leads the gate drew a line under, and the parts of each stage the supervisor is least sure of, each with the check that would settle it. The next stage's refactor-scout reads this file first. A lead a test can settle becomes a test before the next stage's first delegation.

## Stage B1 — supervised-project bootstrap

### Least confident (stage close, 2026-10-16)
1. **The floor's teeth against a real bad dependency.** OSV-Scanner and Socket have run green on the real lockfile, but neither has been seen to fail: no planted known-vulnerable or known-malicious pin has been put through them (Semgrep's teeth were proven with a planted SQL-concat fixture in TASK-002 r2). Check: a throwaway pull request pinning a version with a published advisory in requirements.lock must turn `security floor` red at the OSV step, and one adding a package Socket flags must turn it red at the Socket step; close the pull requests unmerged and delete their branches.
2. **The runner image change.** `ubuntu-latest` is to move to the next Ubuntu image (2026-10-19 by a reviewer's reading during TASK-002, not yet confirmed against GitHub's runner-images notice); the Semgrep step relies on `pipx` being on the image and Node 22 from setup-node. Check: the first `security floor` run after 2026-10-19 shows the Semgrep step running `pipx run semgrep==1.176.1` and the Socket step scanning; if pipx is gone the step fails red (it cannot pass silently, the tests pin its last line).
3. **What Socket's scan actually covers.** The supported-file list shows `requirements.lock` matches, and the scan reports "Found 3 files", unnamed. Check: open the scan of the next `security floor` run in the Socket dashboard (latent-foundry) and confirm pytest, pyyaml and their closure are listed, and which other two files were read.

### Leads (stage B1's review, 2026-10-16)
- The Semgrep rule packs `p/python` and `p/secrets` are fetched from the registry on every run; only the engine is pinned. An upstream rule change can turn an unrelated pull request red or drop coverage unseen. Settle: vendor a dated copy of the two packs into the repository, or accept the drift as a Decision.
- `npm install -g` of the Socket CLI resolves its transitive dependencies afresh on each run (24 packages, no lockfile for a global install). BUG-001 closed the install-time exposure only: whatever of them the CLI loads runs inside `socket ci`, with `SOCKET_CLI_API_TOKEN` in that process's environment, so an unpinned dependency is a risk of BUG-001's kind at scan time. Settle: install the CLI from a committed package-lock in a tool folder (`npm ci --ignore-scripts`), or record the drift as an accepted Decision with its reason; the first changes `test_r002_no_install_runs_beside_a_secret`'s pin, which reads the version on the install line, in the same increment.
- The structural CI test pins the whole workflow verbatim (BUG-001 rounds 5 and 6); its pattern checks remain as a second line that explains what a change breaks, and do not model every install form (pnpm or yarn installs have no script check; an npm option whose value starts with `-`). They matter only when the pin is regenerated: the reviewers of that diff read the workflow change itself.
- The workflow pin is compared through PyYAML, which reads YAML 1.1; GitHub's parser differs on some spellings (`true:`/`yes:`/`On:` for `on:`, a `<<:` merge key, a duplicated key, `off`/`no` for a boolean, `00` for a number), which parse to the pinned values here (BUG-001 round 6, Review A). In each case worked through, the scanners behave the same or GitHub refuses the workflow, so the required checks never report and the merge is blocked. Settle: a throwaway pull request with each spelling, closed unmerged; or compare the raw text as well as the parsed form.
- The scanners' own configuration files (`osv-scanner.toml`, `socket.yml`, `.semgrep.yml`, `bridge/cursor-bridge/semgrep-bridge-rules.yml`, which the workflow passes to Semgrep with `--config`, a new `.semgrepignore`) sit outside the workflow pin; any could narrow a scanner to nothing with every test green (BUG-001 round 6, Review A; main has the same gap). Settle: pin their hashes in the structural test, so a change to one changes the test in the same diff.
- The structural tests guard the merge, not the run: a same-repository pull request that adds a workflow or edits `tests.yml` runs it on its own CI run, with the repository's secrets available, before any test can fail (PR #22, Review A; true on main before). Settle: put SOCKET_SECURITY_API_KEY in a GitHub Environment with required reviewers, or supply it only on pushes to main; either is a change to how the owner's key is reached, so it comes with its run sheet. The cost of each (PR #22, Review A): on pushes only, a pull request's Socket step runs without the key, so it must be skipped there and the required check changes shape; with an Environment, every pull request's CI waits for an approval click.
- The full-tree Semgrep audit (2026-10-16, one accepted finding) is a point-in-time result; diff-aware scans never see code a pull request does not touch. Re-run it at each release (recorded in INVENTORY Decisions).
- The repository secret SEMGREP_APP_TOKEN is stored but unused (OSS engine); the owner could delete it and revoke the token (least privilege; not a defect).

### The stage review, round 2 (2026-10-19)

Snapshot STAGE-B1 (d0199a3 on stage/B1, deleted after verify OK, hash 29df2b69acf62aab), the four squash commits 9e181e6, fba63bc, 6743b25 and 4d0f77b, judged by defect class under standing rule 6. **Review B** (grok-4.7-medium, the native profile): APPROVE, one should-fix (INVENTORY's `--no-banner` row still said the last logs and the key's replacement were asked of the owner) and one consider (the status file's position lines describing their own parent commit). **Review A**: REJECT, Blocking 1 / Should fix 4 / Consider 3; it ran the stage's three test files (23, 8 and 11 passed), the CI-floor driver on a copy (57 of 57 killed), and read main's required checks, a push run and a pull-request run (no banner, the old logs 404, no key-shaped string in the repository).

**Blocking, fixed in BUG-015 (a test claiming more than it checks):** three bootstrap tests checked a word anywhere in a file, where their docstrings claimed a record. Six mutants of the records passed them: the R-038 Decisions row deleted (the Deferred note still names R-038), the decision-6 row deleted (another row names it), the baseline row rewritten as not met, `Installer verification:` deleted, `Merge authority:` blanked, the register's R-038 row deleted (the introduction names the range). test_r001_requirements_register_complete now reads the register's ID column; test_r001_inventory_decisions_recorded reads the Decisions table, each row by its first cell, with an owner (or, for the baseline, the maintainer) and a date in its Settled cell, the baseline as met; test_r001_run_parameters_authoritative requires each of the template's five parameters on one line with one of its values; each reads through a fault function with its own negative test (test_r001_requirements_register_parse, test_r001_inventory_decisions_parse, test_r001_run_parameters_parse), the Decisions one covering the decider, the date and the baseline's "met" as well as the row. All six mutants are killed, each by the test it names (run/supervisor/bug015_mutants.py, on a copy; Review A of BUG-015 asked for the register's negative test and the named kills). Round 1's should-fix ("the bootstrap records' fields checked") had tightened the Deferred and phase tests and left these three.

**Should-fix and considers, taken in BUG-015 (records):** INVENTORY's `--no-banner` row brought to the 2026-10-18 outcome (Review A and Review B, converging); the status file's position lines rewritten with this change; project.json's `version` is the bridge's, 2026.10.18a; the status's list of B1's change names all four commits; R-001's evidence names BUG-001 and BUG-015.

**Should-fix, recorded (rule 6: the class of the lead above, "an unpinned dependency is a risk of BUG-001's kind at scan time"):** the key is apart from other code by step, not by job. The `security floor` job runs `pipx run semgrep==1.176.1`, whose dependency ranges are open (attrs, glom, rich, ruamel.yaml, packaging, requests, urllib3, read from PyPI's metadata by Review A), before the Socket step; code run there can reach the Socket step's key by appending to `$GITHUB_PATH` (a `socket` ahead of npm's), to `$GITHUB_ENV` (a `BASH_ENV` the step's bash reads), or by leaving a process alive that reads the scan's environment. BUG-001's mechanism holds (the keyless `--ignore-scripts` install runs no package code), and INVENTORY's "the key is in the scan's step only" is true of the step, not of the job. Settle: move the Socket install and scan into a job of their own (checkout, setup-node, install, scan), so the key's job runs only the Socket CLI's code, changing the workflow pin and test_r002_one_step_holds_a_secret in the same diff; a builder increment through the gate, its Scope the workflow and the CI-floor tests. The new job keeps the name `security floor`, which main's rules require (the owner's decision of 2026-10-13), or the owner adds the new job's name to the required checks by a run sheet before the merge (superseded by round 3's record below: the Socket job takes a name of its own, added to the required checks); otherwise the Socket scan would stop gating merges (Review A of BUG-015). Confirm first, without the key: a throwaway pull request whose Semgrep step appends a folder to `$GITHUB_PATH`, then `which socket` in the next step. The key is Socket's free tier, with no spend possible.

**Recorded:** least-confident item 2 (the runner image): the pull-request run of 2026-10-19 12:14 UTC (39250543953) still ran on `ubuntu-24.04`, image 20261012.334.1, with Semgrep and Socket working; not settled until a run on the new image. Review A's assumption, not checked: that a fork's pull request makes the Socket step fail, rather than fall back, when `SOCKET_CLI_API_TOKEN` is empty; harmless, since fork pull requests are not expected.

### The stage review, round 3 (2026-10-19)

Snapshot STAGE-B1 (2dbf9d6 on stage/B1, deleted after verify OK, hash d8590ae43a38aec4), the five squash commits 9e181e6, fba63bc, 6743b25, 4d0f77b and 1b8c8da (BUG-015, PR #44), judged by defect class under standing rule 6. **Review B** (grok-4.7-medium, the native profile): REJECT on one finding; it ran the stage's three test files (45 passed) and the full suite (1618 passed, 24 skipped). **Review A**: APPROVE, Blocking 0 / Should fix 1 / Consider 4; it ran the stage's tests on a copy (45 passed), twelve record mutants (round 2's six killed, each by its named test) and eleven workflow mutants on the CI-floor test (all killed), and read main's push run 39259896507 (both test legs and the security floor green, no Socket banner line, no key-shaped string) and main's required checks.

**Blocking, fixed in BUG-016 (convergent: Review B's blocking finding, Review A's should-fix):** the status file on main described its own pull request before the gate (BUG-015 on its branch, "before its gate", Last accepted and Remote sync at the previous main commit, Next the gate already run), and REQUIREMENTS' R-001 evidence named BUG-015 without its commit. It is round 1's blocking class and round 2's consider, which BUG-015 recorded as fixed: the file is written inside each pull request before the gate, and a squash puts it on main unchanged (ISS-009). BUG-016 writes the file as it reads at its own merge (Last accepted names the pull request first, by number; In flight none; Next the step after the merge) and adds test_r001_status_names_its_own_merge, which checks that in CI from GitHub's own variables (the merge ref of a pull request; the squash subject of a push to main) and skips locally, with test_r001_status_position_parse as its negative test. Run with main's push variables against main's file at 1b8c8da, the guard fails.

**Considers, taken in BUG-016:** the status file was at its cap (11,985 of 12,000): the spent sections (the re-plan, stage B2's close, BUG-015 before its gate) pruned. The run parameters' values were not fixed in a test (rule 52; Review A's probes flipped Parallel increments and Merge authority and passed all eleven tests): test_r001_run_parameters_owner_values fixes the owner's four answers, and INVENTORY's run-parameter row now points at RUN_PARAMETERS.md instead of repeating it. DESIGN_CRITIQUE.md's "gets R-039" for the install-mechanics decision annotated: it is R-038.

**Consider, recorded (rule 6: the class of the leads above, "an unpinned dependency is a risk of BUG-001's kind at scan time"):** `node-version: "22"` in the `security floor` job lets whichever Node 22 release the setup action serves run the Socket CLI with the key. Settle with the job split of round 2's settle path: the Socket job pins an exact Node release.

**Consider on round 2's settle path (Review A of BUG-015's last commits):** splitting the Socket install and scan into a job of their own leaves two jobs where one is required; two jobs both named `security floor` would leave unclear which one satisfies the rule, and a green Semgrep run could count while Socket fails. The settle path is therefore: the Socket job takes a name of its own, and the owner adds that name to main's required checks by a run sheet before the split merges.

**Review A of BUG-016 (APPROVE 0/1/5), taken in BUG-016:** the guard judged a pull request into any branch (now only one into main, GITHUB_BASE_REF); In flight is `none` alone and Increment begins `none`; a pull request into main whose number cannot be read fails rather than skips; the status's run-parameter line a pointer only; the convention's costs written in the status file and ISS-009; round 2's settle path annotated. Not taken: a check of the Next line, whose text names the step after the merge in free prose; reviewers read it.

**Recorded, kept:** the Decisions reader matches the decider as a word in the Settled cell ("2026-10-11, supervisor (owner not asked)" passes); BUG-015's Review A consider 2, kept: the cell's form is free text, and who decided is rule 51's record, read by the reviewers.

No REJECT, so no round 5 (the stage close's step 7).

R-002's evidence named "its round-6 follow-up" without its commit: 4d0f77b added. **Recorded (Feedback FB-024 and the least-confident item 3 below):** during a gate the status file's In flight reads `none` by the convention, so a resume finds the live position only through the file's "How this file is written" pointer to run/supervisor/in-flight.md; and a settle path's throwaway pull request now fails the tests job on the status guard, which its run sheet must say.

### Least confident (stage close, 2026-10-19)
1. **The status guard's push path rests on GitHub's squash subject.** test_r001_status_names_its_own_merge reads the pull request's number on a push to main from the head commit's subject ending `(#n)`; a merge made any other way (a merge commit, an edited subject) skips silently. Check: at each stage close, the push run of the last squash reports, on each leg, the same passed and skipped counts as that pull request's last run on the same tree (a skipped guard shows one passed fewer and one skipped more); today PR #45's run 39267616332 and main's push run 39268304307 agree, ubuntu 1619 passed / 26 skipped and windows 1622 / 23. CI's `pytest -q` prints no per-file counts, so the comparison is per leg. A later increment may make the push path fail rather than skip on a subject without `(#n)`, as the pull-request path already does.
2. **The Socket key's job also runs code it does not pin.** Semgrep's open dependency ranges (round 2's lead) and `node-version: "22"` (round 3's) run in the job whose last step holds the key; BUG-001's keyless `--ignore-scripts` install holds, so no mechanism is defeated. Check: the job split of round 2's settle path, the Socket job with its own name and an exact Node release, its name added to main's required checks by the owner's run sheet before it merges; first the throwaway probe that appends to `$GITHUB_PATH` and runs `which socket` in the next step; that probe's tests job fails on the status guard by design (FB-024), so the probe is read in its own step and in `security floor`.
3. **A resume during a gate depends on a file a clone does not have.** Between merges the live position is in run/supervisor/in-flight.md (gitignored); the session-start hook points at the status file, whose In flight reads `none`. Check: after the next compaction during a gate, the resumed session names the gate in progress before its first action; the lasting fix is the hook naming the scratch log (FB-022), a bridge change for the maintainer, or B3's module work if it reaches the hook.

## Stage B3 — the core and phase-module split

### CFG-B3, the builder's configuration (2026-10-19)
- **Lead (ISS-010, FB-025): the boundary-check hook does not run in builder runs.** The project `afterFileEdit` hooks never fired in four headless cursor-agent runs launched the bridge's way (Git Bash, the agent's own home), with or without the matcher, with `python` or the interpreter's full path, with `--trust`; the hook's command run by hand works. The cause (the CLI not running project afterFileEdit hooks headless, or composing them for a shell Git Bash cannot run) is not separated: a launch outside Git Bash would settle it, which the bridge does not make. Until then the Workspace boundary rests on the always-on rule, which an explicit prompt overrode in the probe, and on the supervisor's outside-write check at step 4 (a marker before the launch, then `find` over the project root; INVENTORY Decisions), which sees the project root only; step 4's reading of `docs/BOUNDARY_VIOLATIONS.md` finds nothing because nothing writes it. Settle: the maintainer's launch-mode probe (FB-025), or a detector in the launcher.
- **Recorded:** the pre-commit gate runs only on this computer (`core.hooksPath` is a local setting); CI runs the windowless check over bridge/cursor-bridge only (.github/workflows/tests.yml line 26), so a clone without the setting is gated at the pull request for the bridge's programs but not for .cursor/hooks: its three files are byte copies of programs CI does scan, and an edit to a copy alone would pass CI unscanned (adding the folder to the CI step is a workflow change, for the next increment that touches the workflow). **Closed by CONFIG-B3b** (PR #49): CI's windowless step scans .cursor/hooks too (.github/workflows/tests.yml, the step `windowless check over the bridge's programs`).

### FIX-001, the test suite's launches windowless (2026-10-19)
- **Why now:** at 17:59 UTC on 2026-10-19 the owner's computer hard-crashed after a flood of terminal windows, while the CONFIG-B3b round-3 builder ran the full suite (17:52:57-17:58:28) and a mutation loop over tests/test_dump_cli.py, and another project's mutation run and builder ran beside it; Windows logged ephemeral TCP port exhaustion at 17:54:25. Which program opened the windows is not proven; the owner switched on Windows' process-start recording. The supervisor found the mechanism on its side and the maintainer measured its extent: 32 of the 34 test files that start programs passed no window flag (test_bridge_run.py 28 sites, test_workflow_check.py 15, test_a4_followups.py 13, test_roster_check.py 9, ...), so a suite run from a process with no console gives every launch a visible window (KP-014). The supervisor's own suite runs were not exposed: its shell holds a console with no window (GetConsoleProcessList 3, GetConsoleWindow 0), which children inherit.
- **The fix:** one default in tests/conftest.py, the Popen wrapper (INVENTORY Decisions); in its first commit five tests in tests/test_tests_windowless.py, all red against the checkpoint's conftest.py (verified on a copy) and green with it (nine after the review rounds below).
- **Review A's round (APPROVE 0/3/7), all three should-fix taken:** (1) the console checks counted processes ("0 1", "0 2"), which a virtual environment's launcher python.exe makes "0 4": they now name processes (the test's own process not on the child's console; the child on the grandchild's), green from Review A's venv; (2) a launch that redirects none of its streams lost its output to the new hidden console, and a read of its input would wait there: such a launch also gets stdin=DEVNULL, so Python hands it the suite's own output handles (a test with capfd, red against the first version of the fix: it timed out); (3) the INVENTORY row said "every program": it now says every launch that sets no creation flags, and a test fails on a launch path that bypasses subprocess or on explicit flags without CREATE_NO_WINDOW in tests/. Eight tests then.
- **Review A's round 2 (REJECT 1/0/5):** the detector could not fail (its regex emptied, its flags rule dropped or its loop emptied, it still passed). Now one per-line rule with a positive control (twelve known ways around the default, among them the forms Review A's notes listed: `creationflags = …` with spaces, `from os import system`, a `**{"creationflags": …}` key, a comment naming CREATE_NO_WINDOW, ProcessPoolExecutor) and a negative one, and the scan must have read test_bridge_run.py, test_workflows_line_check.py and helpers/sleep_tree.py (subfolders now walked) and seen a flags line; six mutants of the detector, each red (the supervisor's driver on a scratch copy). Nine tests.
- **Review A's round 3 (APPROVE 0/1/3), the should-fix taken:** nothing showed that a finding inside a scanned file reached the result (`if False:` in place of the rule's call survived). The walk is now `scan(folder)`, and a test plants a bypass in a subfolder of a temporary folder (and one in a conftest.py, which is skipped) and asserts the one line reported; nine mutants of the detector, each red. Ten tests.
- **Lead (Review A, noted):** the detector reads lines, not the syntax tree: a launch split over lines (flags on one line, CREATE_NO_WINDOW on the next) is flagged wrongly, which fails loudly; a launch reached through a name bound elsewhere (`run = os.system`) is not seen; a `#` inside a string literal cuts the line short and could hide flags after it (no launch line in tests/ has one); a fixture under tests/ holding a bypass as data would fail the scan, loudly (exclude fixtures/ then). Check: an AST scan if any of these forms appears.
- **Lead (Review A, noted):** each flagless launch now starts a hidden console host of its own, where under an inherited console it started none: hundreds to thousands of short-lived processes more per suite run. Check: the suite's time before and after (the launcher's run log: the four full-suite runs of 2026-10-19 before the fix took 293 to 320 s; FIX-001's first run, 1625 passed, took 302 s: no cost seen).
- **Lead (seen in passing, older than FIX-001):** the suite's own launches through bridge-run.py write into the Workspace's real run/launcher/runs.jsonl (1266 entries of kind test, limit 30, 0 to 1 s, healthy or exit 7). A ceiling is learned from the longest healthy run, so these raise none, but they fill the log a resume and a ceiling read. Check: which tests launch with the Workspace as the launcher's run folder, and give them a temporary one.
- **Lead:** the builder's own process tree (cursor-agent's shell and what it starts outside pytest) is not covered by this fix; the owner's recording at the next builder run shows whether anything else opens windows.
- **Lead:** test_bridge_run.py::test_an_orphan_left_at_a_normal_end_is_killed failed once in CONFIG-B3b round 1's builder run ("orphan survived") and passed on a rerun; no earlier record. Watch for a recurrence before diagnosing (root-cause-first needs a reproduction).

### CONFIG-B3b, the generated command reference (2026-10-19)
- **Rounds:** four builder rounds (composer-2.5). Round 1: a list default leaked a path and a path on another drive crashed the generator (both reviewers). Round 2: the path check read a default through `json.dumps`, which doubles backslashes, so on Windows nothing matched (both reviewers, blocking), and the Markdown list markers were lost. Round 3: the walk and raw-string check landed; the security re-check failed it because a folder was found in one written form only (doubled backslashes from a repr or JSON in a help text, the Git Bash and WSL forms, no drive letter), REVIEWERS DISAGREE (Review A approved without trying those forms) decided for the security side by rule 54; and a dict default with tuple keys crashed (convergent: supervisor, security, Review A). Round 4: one canonical form, each folder with and without its drive, distinct string keys, NaN refused, choices walked, the type field checked. CFG-B3's recorded gap is closed: CI's windowless step now scans .cursor/hooks.
- **Decided (round 4's builder, accepted):** an absolute default or choice equal to the program's folder itself is refused (its relative path would be "."), reconciling round 4's "choices under the folder take the placeholder" with round 3's test of a choice equal to the folder. No program has such a default today (the reference is byte-identical). Lead: a future `--root` defaulting to its own folder could not be documented, and the refusal says "outside the bridge folder", which is wrong for the folder itself. Check: write the folder itself as `<bridge folder>` and give the case its own test, when such an option appears.
- **Lead (Review A round 4, S1, should-fix, fails closed):** the fragment without the drive refuses a clean reference when one of the three folders is one level deep and its name occurs in the reference (measured: a repository at `D:\docs`, `D:\workflow`, `D:\work`, `D:\main` or `D:\p` is refused; `D:\src`, `D:\code`, `D:\dev` are fine); the same for a current or home folder of that shape. Not hit today (this checkout, the home folder, both CI runners). The refusal does not say which folder matched. Check: compare the drive-less fragment only with two components or more, match at a component boundary, and name the folder kind (repository, home or current) in the message; the last is worth taking whenever tools/dump-cli.py is next touched, so a false positive explains itself.
- **Lead (security re-check, round 4 advisories, contrived):** a URL-encoded Windows path (`C%3A%5CUsers%5C<name>`) and a path with a `.` folder in it (`C:\Users\.\<name>`) pass; a type callable whose `__name__` is not a string reaches the reference unchecked when JSON can hold it (an int) and ends in a traceback when it cannot, and an `nargs` changed after `add_argument` reaches it unchecked; a non-ASCII user name escaped by `json.dumps` (`\u00e9`) is not matched. Check: compare `urllib.parse.unquote(text)` too, `os.path.normpath` path-like parts, write the type only when its name is a string, check nargs as a string.
- **Lead (security re-check, out of the brief's scope):** only the repository, home and current folders are checked; text naming another folder of this computer (the repository's parent, another drive) passes; none carries the user's name. A link placed at one of the two output files would be followed (git here has `core.symlinks=false`). Check: refuse when either output is a link (`os.path.islink`); widen the folder list if a program ever names another.
- **Lead (round 2, C6):** a default derived from the environment that is not a path (a user name read from `USERNAME`, say) cannot be told from a constant and would be written. Check: none planned; no program has one.
- **Lead (round 2):** the pre-commit `--check` reads the working tree, not the index, so a commit that stages a stale reference while the working copy is fresh passes locally; CI's check catches it at the pull request. Check: run the check on `git stash --keep-index` state if it ever matters.
- **Lead (Review B, the records round 2, noted):** the Windows 8.3 short-name form of a folder (`C:\Users\ABCDEF~1`, which Windows can give a folder whose name is longer than eight characters) is not matched, so a program naming its folder that way would pass. The likely source is the temporary folder (`%TEMP%` and `tempfile.gettempdir()` often hold the short form, whose first six characters come from the user's name) and a current folder started in that form; no bridge program or tools/dump-cli.py calls `gettempdir` (Review A, FIX-002). Check: compare `GetLongPathNameW` of path-like parts too, if a program ever defaults to the temporary folder.
- **Lead (Review A round 4, noted):** the filesystem-root skip in `forbidden_path_fragments` is now close to dead code (the canonical `/` skip covers it off Windows; `c:/` never appears in the reference), and the root tests cannot fail on either leg; on Linux the three drive-less cases test the separator folding only, the drive mechanism being exercised on the Windows leg alone.

### TASK-016, the traceability check (2026-10-20)
- **Rounds:** four builder rounds (composer-2.5). Round 1 traced the 424 passages right (Review A checked them independently) and was rejected for chained citations unchecked, a traceback at `git ls-tree`, findings out of order and an entry's line number off by one, and failed by the security auditor: a `~/.claude/` reference was not contained in `bridge/` (a `..`, drive or network form probed outside it). Round 2 contained references; Review A then found a Windows junction under `workflow/` followed by `os.walk` (round 2's brief had wrongly said it is not), MAP homes unescaped (convergent with the security re-check) and references sorted by text. Round 3 refused links below the walk's start and refused `...` by mistake (its brief's trailing-dot rule); both reviewers then found the start folder, the folders above it and the three files read by name unchecked (a junction at `workflow/` or `bridge/commands` printed outside text; convergent). Round 4: one helper checks every component of every path read, a link is `ERROR`, exit 2. Review A APPROVE 0/0/5, the security re-check PASS; a by-class line drawn on the leads below (step 7).
- **Lead (security re-check, rounds 3 and 4, agreed as leads):** a race between the link test and the read: a path swapped for a link between `lstat` and `open()` is read, and `bridge_contains` calls `realpath` after the component walk, which follows a link swapped in meanwhile. Local only, and whoever can swap a path during a run can edit the files the check reads. Check: open with no-follow (`O_NOFOLLOW`; `FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` on Windows) and compare `fstat` with the earlier `lstat`.
- **Lead (security re-check, round 4):** a hard link is not a link to the check: a `.md` under `workflow/` hard-linked to a file elsewhere on the drive is read. git never creates hard links on checkout. Check: refuse a file with `st_nlink > 1`.
- **Lead (security re-check, round 4):** the refusals of a linked file (a `.md` under `workflow/`, the three files read by name) are proven only on CI: this Windows account cannot make a symbolic link to a file, so those four tests are skipped here; the code tests the last component of every path the same way. Check: CI's run of tests/test_trace_check.py, which skips none of them on Linux.
- **Lead (Review A round 4, consider):** with two or more links under `workflow/`, which one the ERROR names follows `os.walk`'s listing order (NTFS name order on Windows, the file system's hash order on Linux), so two computers can print different ERROR lines for one tree; the exit (2) is the same. Check: sort `dirnames` and `filenames` in place before testing them.
- **Lead (Review A round 4, consider; unreachable from main):** a reference whose first segment is a placeholder (`~/.claude/.../x`, `~/.claude/<x>/y`) never tests that `bridge/` exists, so `resolve_ref` answers yes on a root without `bridge/`; `main` reads the core under `bridge/` first, so it cannot happen there. Check: an `lstat` of `bridge/` if `resolve_ref` is reused.
- **Lead (Review A round 4, consider):** the docstring's exit-2 list names "git unavailable" but not a git command that times out or fails after it started (`ERROR: git ls-tree: timed out after <n> s`), which also ends in exit 2.
- **Lead (Review A round 4, noted):** the walk tests each entry with `is_path_link` rather than the whole-path helper (equivalent: the walk is top-down from a folder already checked); `resolve_ref` walks a reference's components twice (the helper answers "no link" for a missing component too, so existence is walked again); an unreadable folder's ERROR prints the absolute path where a link's prints the repository-relative one.
- **Lead (Review A round 3, noted):** the device names refused are the brief's (`CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `COM1`-`COM9`, `LPT1`-`LPT9`), narrower than Python 3.13's reserved set (`CONIN$`, `CONOUT$`, `COM` and `LPT` with a superscript digit); on this computer `lstat` fails for all of those, so they do not resolve anyway.
- **Accepted (mutation (ab), the builder's assumption):** the link test of the walk's start folder is shadowed by the test of the baseline file, whose path runs through the same folder and is checked first; it stays as the brief's guard. Reopens if a file read by name moves out of `workflow/`.
- **For TASK-010 (Review A round 1's design point):** an exceptions entry names a file and a heading, so an entry naming a level-1 heading or the digest's heading excuses every passage under it; the design review of each split increment reads every entry's heading for that reach.

### TASK-010 with TASK-015, the carve and the hook's module pointers (2026-10-20)
- **The carve (TASK-010, the supervisor's governance text):** every phase section moved verbatim from bridge/commands/supervisor.md into its module under workflow/phases/, project-end carved out of the reflection points as a declared split; trace-check.py: 424 passages of baseline 0a186da, 157 in the core, 263 under workflow/, 0 dropped, 4 excused (three passages of "Main and conditional workflows" under its TASK-010 reword entry, the layering table's among them since the gate's round 2; one TASK-015 reword of rule 28).
- **Rounds (TASK-015, composer-2.5):** seven builder rounds, each opened by the supervisor's tests, red against the round before. Round 1: a path printed twice, a part silenced by a checker failure, folder links followed (Review A). Round 2: a checker that exits, a path the checker returned printed, the intact precondition unshown (convergent). Round 3: a reason with a line break, an exception's type name, the link test and the read reaching different files (convergent). Round 4: a `..` after a linked folder read another file than the link test tested, on Linux (convergent). Round 5: reading a type name could itself raise (Review A REJECT, convergent with the security audit), a str subclass printing its `__str__`; and the release stamp left stale, which failed 39 install tests and errored 9. Round 6: pointer lines took a str subclass, a BaseException escaped (convergent). Round 7: Review A APPROVE 0/2/4, both should-fix the supervisor's (a plain-subclass case for R7-1, a driver label), taken; the security audit PASS with four advisories.
- **By-class line (step 7), drawn after round 7:** the tail is a checker written to defeat the hook, which the docstring puts out of scope ("not a hostile checker, which runs in-process and could write to its output directly"). Review A and the security audit both named the first two leads below, each placing them outside the threat model. The merge policy's convergence rule (Merge-Verification-Policy.md, "Convergence is a signal": two reviewers naming the same defect make it blocking "even if each alone marked it advisory") admits no exception for a threat model, so this line departs from its wording; the supervisor takes the by-class rule's reading (step 7: the long tail of one hard subsystem goes to this file unless it is a new class or defeats a safety mechanism the increment relies on), since the hook's docstring already declares the class, every further guard narrows a channel an in-process program can open another way, and the second lead's fix (ending the process before the checker's objects are freed, or a child process) is a new mechanism, not a correction. The first lead's fix is one line and is taken at the hook's next edit. The gap between the two rules is a Feedback entry owed (PROJECT_STATUS.md). A finding inside the model (a partial, mismatched or hand-edited install) still blocks, unless nothing can reach it yet: the fence lead below is latent (no module has a pointer line) and accepted until the first pointer line, whose increment fixes it first.
- **Lead (both reviewers, round 7):** the exception's type name is read in the `except` handler, after the redirection ends (session-start.py, `load_checker_module` and the use handler of `pointers_part`), so a metaclass whose `__name__` prints writes to the hook's output. Check: call `safe_exc_type_name` inside a short redirection of its own. **Closed by FIX-003** (the redirection is inside `safe_exc_type_name`; test_r005_the_type_name_is_read_where_the_checker_cannot_print).
- **Lead (both reviewers, round 7):** a `__del__` on checker objects freed when the handler ends, an `atexit` callback the checker registers, or a thread it starts runs after the redirection, after the hook's own write, uncapped. Check: flush and end with `os._exit(0)` after the write, or run the checker in a child process with its output captured. FIX-003's security audit adds one case: a type name that is a str subclass is refused, but freed only when `safe_exc_type_name` returns, after its redirection ends, so its `__del__` runs unredirected (as does `err`'s after the handlers); when this lead is taken, `del name` inside the `with` as well.
- **Lead (the security audit, round 7):** the redirection covers writes through `sys.stdout` and `sys.stderr` only; `sys.__stdout__`, `os.write(1, ...)` and a child process inheriting the streams pass it. The docstring's "what the checker prints is discarded" reads wider than that (INVENTORY's row names the two streams). Check: the docstring names the two streams at the next edit of the hook. **The docstring part closed by FIX-003** ("what the checker prints through sys.stdout and sys.stderr is discarded"); the channels themselves stay outside the model.
- **Lead (the security audit, round 7):** the discard buffers are unbounded; a checker printing in a loop grows memory rather than being cut. Check: a bounded sink if a checker ever prints.
- **Lead (Review A, round 7 and the gate; inside the model, latent, accepted until the first pointer line):** the exact-str test of R7-1 runs before the fence logic. A mismatched checker whose `file_lines` returns lines of another type (bytes, say) has every line skipped: no pointer-line file is named and no cannot-be-used line says so, against the feature's "one plain line" (INVENTORY Features). Nothing is affected today, since no module has a `Sub-files:` or `Variants:` line; a checker returning mixed lines would also skip a non-str opening fence and read a pointer line inside it. Check, before TASK-012 or TASK-013 adds the first pointer line: a non-str line from `file_lines` raises inside the handler (so the part says `cannot be used`), with a test of the uniform case. **Closed by FIX-003** (`raise TypeError` before the fence logic; test_r005_a_line_that_is_not_a_str_makes_the_checker_unusable[uniform|fence]).
- **Lead (Review A, round 7, an assumption the builder did not list; inside the model, accepted as failing closed):** the checker is loaded under a `StringIO` standard output, so a checker that touches `sys.stdout.buffer` or calls `reconfigure` at import fails to load (`cannot be loaded (AttributeError)`, closed); workflow-check.py does both only under its `__main__` guard today.
- **Lead (rounds 1 to 4):** `dont_write_bytecode` stops the hook writing a `.pyc` but not reading one: a cached `__pycache__` copy of workflow-check.py whose recorded source mtime and size match the source is loaded instead of it, which inside the model means an edit of the same size within the same second, or a restore that keeps the old mtime. The installer writes none. Check: load with `SourceFileLoader` from the source text, or refuse a `__pycache__` under the bridge folder.
- **Lead (rounds 3 and 4):** between the link test and the read a path can be swapped for a link (the same race as TASK-016's lead); whoever can swap it can edit the files the hook reads. Check: open with no-follow and compare `fstat` with the `lstat`.
- **Lead (round 2; inside the model, as version skew, accepted until a grammar change):** `parse_workflows_tail` re-implements the Workflows line's grammar beside workflow-check.py's own judge (`check_status`, `prefix_of`); a grammar change made in one would leave the other. Check: the hook calls the checker's parser once the checker exports one for a single line (a refactoring candidate for stage B3's pass; judged at the pass, 2026-10-22, not a candidate: the checker exports no parser for a single line, its `check_status` reads a `<part>: ` prefix and unstripped text differently, and the hook parses before it decides whether to load the checker at all, so delegating would change which files are named and when the not-loadable line shows; it stays the version-skew lead).
- **Recorded:** the root-link test in `conditional_path_accessible` is redundant since R3-3 (a linked workflow/ stops the tables first) and kept as defence in depth (the driver's r2-4-workflow-root-link-dropped survives as labelled); three mutants are equivalent (f, r, r3-4); three are killed only where a file link can be made (h and the two r3-3 mutants: the link tests skip on this account and run on Linux CI).
- **Accepted (Review A round 6, C3):** a table key that is not an exact str is skipped like a key outside the key pattern, with no cannot-read line; before round 6 a non-string key raised TypeError and printed `cannot be used (TypeError)`. The checker validates the keys; a key of another type is a crafted table, and naming no file fails closed. Reopens if a checker returns keys of another type by design.
- **Lead (seen at this compaction, a usability defect):** the hook reads any `Awaiting user on:` value other than nothing, none, empty or `-` as a wait, so "none (resumed 2026-10-20 on the owner's word)" told the resumed supervisor to wait. Check: the status file writes `nothing` (done at this merge); a hook that reads only the field's first word is a bridge feedback lead.
- **Taken at the gate (Review A, the hook's part):** the no-bytecode test passed with bytecode writing switched off by the runner's environment (PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE), so it proved nothing on this computer: the test helper now drops the variable and the test shows the checker was loaded; the replace clause of Requirement 5 had no test: one added. ### TASK-013, the build-phase modules and the delegation loop's split (2026-10-20)

- **The split:** delegation-loop.md was 56,457 characters (94% of R-032's 60,000). Round 1's additions came to 2,967 characters, which would have left it at about 59,165 unsplit (98.6%, inside the bound), so the split rested then on TASK-010's recorded decision that an enrichment adding to the module splits it first (INVENTORY, Decisions, the core's size); with round 2's additions the unsplit module would be about 60,500, past the bound itself. Its step 9, the stage-close refactoring pass, moved verbatim into delegation-loop/refactoring-pass.md, named by the module's `Sub-files:` line, the first real one; the module is 54,965 characters (92%) and the sub-file 6,515. - **Round 1: security auditor FAIL, Review A REJECT**, every blocking and should-fix item taken, and two findings convergent (a widened caller had no path; the homes of a secret were not N2's and KP-032's). The audit's two blocking items: the contract text named caller classes without the mechanism that tells them apart, so a local API open to "any local program" would be reachable from every web page the owner's browser opens and "only the own front end" would rest on an Origin claim; and nothing bounded what an operation's inputs may reach, which leaves path traversal and another caller's records open, the MCP case worst since its caller is a model a document can steer. Review A's blocking item: the stand-in guard never showed its patterns match anything (TASK-012's class, again). The audit's seven advisories were taken too.

### TASK-018, the new trait files (2026-10-24)
- **Checkpoint run (step 4):** tests/test_traits_new.py and tests/test_b3_least_confident.py run once against b03fd42 in a read-only worktree: 28 failed, 78 passed; the new file's one passing test there is the guard that the workflow check passes over workflow/, which holds on both sides; the eleven new pins fail and the earlier pins pass.
- **Round 1: Review A REJECT (2/5/3), the secret sentinel CLEAR.** Both blocking items taken: the dry run needs recorded replies, which a first contract capture does not yet have, so a capture's first paid run and a paid action with no remote service run at the smallest size under their own approval, counted beforehand; and the store of paid results reached neither the builder nor the gate, so every paid call the part's code makes goes through it (a brief constraint) and a paid call that bypasses it is a blocking finding (the inspect check). The five should-fixes taken: the provider's usage read only where a Resources row records a way, else by the owner at the stage close; the approval covers the loop's paid actions, the product enforcing the cap in the owner's use as a value fixed in a test (rule 52); the process's commands recorded in the increment that builds them; a model judge pinned and its verdicts recorded; the design's Evaluation section stating the test step's block. Two considers taken: the copy check reads the plan critic and the other trait files; the N1 guard reads each of the eight conditions. The third (the workflow-check test holds on both sides) is accepted: a guard on the state, as the checkpoint run says.
- **Lead (Review A, TASK-018, intent):** the open list defines *long-running process* as keeping a process running between uses, while N1's fourth condition says a background process stops when idle. The trait binds the runs the loop starts (a test, a demonstration, a check) to N1, as §6.8's "the conditions of 8.1" says, and leaves the product's own lifetime to its design. Check: TASK-020's local service, which the owner keeps running, states its lifetime in its type file; if the owner wants N1's idle stop for a product's service too, that is the owner's question at TASK-020's design, not this trait's.
- **Round 2: Review A APPROVE (0/3/1),** every round-1 finding fixed at its cause; the three new should-fixes and the consider taken: the intake notes carry spends money's two duties to the design (how the product enforces the cap; the store's folder), since the trait adds no design section; the inspect check fails a paid call that does not both read the store and write to it; a model judge's verdicts are judged again when the code that shapes the judged result changes; a run a test starts and stops is part of that test, so N1's fifth condition (no gate depends on it being up) reads as no gate depending on a process the test did not start.
- **Review B, round 1: REJECT, two findings, both taken.** The nothing-left-running check would have stopped the instance the owner runs (it named every process the part runs, and the procedure's stop command stops them all): a test now starts an instance of its own, on a port and data of its own, and the check stops only what started during the tests, by itself and its children, leaving the owner's process as it is. A model judge's re-judging could be skipped where it costs money, and the evaluation then read a stale pass: until new verdicts are recorded, a change whose judged result changed is blocked at the test step. Review A had read both sections twice (convergence did not hold here; the cross-family read caught what the Anthropic one missed).
- **Review B, round 2: APPROVE; Review A's read of the round-1 fixes: APPROVE, two should-fix and two consider, all taken.** The start and stop commands take the port and the data of the instance they start and stop, so that the brief's test instance can run beside the owner's; the nothing-left-running check tells what the tests started by descent from the test run or by a test instance's port or data, never by start time alone (which would catch another session's process), stops it by its process ID with its children or by the stop command given that instance's port and data, and leaves the owner's and another session's processes as they are; each model judge's verdict is kept with the judged result it read, so that a changed result shows by comparison, and where a model costs money the block waits on the run the owner approved, which the supervisor starts, never on a re-delegation. Review B reads the change again.
- **Review A's confirming read of ce44062: APPROVE, two considers, kept as leads.** (1) A test instance told by its port or data cannot be told from another session's instance on the same fixed port, and the two collide there: the brief constraint could have each run take a port and a data folder of its own (a free port chosen at start); a lead for TASK-020's local service. (2) A judged result that varies between identical runs (a timestamp, an id, an unordered collection) differs from the kept one on every change: the comparison could read a normalised form, or the design's Evaluation section name what it ignores; a lead for the first project with the trait AI models.
- **Review B, round 3: APPROVE,** on the whole change after Review A's four points; merged as PR #69.

### BUG-020, the loop guard's cron_next in the repeated hour (2026-10-25)
- **Reproduction:** tests/test_loop_guard.py::test_bug020_a_one_shot_in_the_repeated_hour_is_found, on Europe/Berlin through the new `tz` argument, five cases; red on the checkpoint b5453bd (TypeError on `tz`), green on the fix; it skips where the computer has no zone data, so on such a computer only the live-clock test sees the fold.
- **The local zone on Windows in the repeated hour (Review A's should-fix):** the accept commit 5ebcfe3 cited the live-clock test passing at 01:38 UTC as evidence, but that test's target, now + 1,800 s, was 02:08 UTC, outside the repeated hour, so it proved nothing. The evidence instead: on this computer `datetime.fromtimestamp` with no zone reads 00:49, 01:09, 01:49 and 02:09 UTC of 2026-10-25 as 02:49 (fold 0), 02:09 (fold 1), 02:49 (fold 1) and 03:09; and the fixed `cron_next` with no zone returns 01:09 UTC for `9 2 25 10 *` from 00:35 UTC and 01:49 UTC for `49 2 25 10 *` from 01:20 UTC (both probes 2026-10-25, supervisor and Review A independently). The reproduction test covers the named-zone path only; the no-zone path meets the fold only on the night itself.
- **Lead (the security audit, advisory, older than this fix):** `cron_field` raises on a step of 0 (`*/0`, ZeroDivisionError) and on a non-ASCII digit or a number of more than 4,300 digits (ValueError from `int()`), and the call of `cron_next` in `pending_from_hook` catches neither, so the hook's top level exits 0: it fails open, but one malformed session cron switches the whole guard off for that stop, the background-agent check included; the fix is a malformed field counting as no match, or the entry counted among the unknown crons. For a later fix increment.
- **Lead:** the `timedelta` import of loop-guard.py is used by no code since the fix (the brief kept every line outside `cron_next`); for the next refactoring pass. Landed: REFAC-002 (stage B4's pass).
- **The builder's report** carried this computer's Workspace path once; replaced by `<the Workspace>` before the commit (rule 54).

## Stage B2 — carried to its close (test lane)
- Done on BUG-002 (stage B2's review fixes), not in the refactoring pass, whose test lane only consolidates: tests/test_open_list.py pins "filled, including" and its comment at :25 is corrected (TASK-004 r3 Consider); tests/test_extension_points.py pins the configuration.items CI route, the inspect step's members and the placement sentence (TASK-005 r6 Consider).

## Stage B2 — TASK-006, the workflow check (by-class line, review round 3)
- A change word wrapped in markup or joined by another character is still prose: `## **Set** intake.x`, `` ## `Replace` design ``, `## Set:at intake.x`, `## Set` with an em dash. Strip leading and trailing `*`, `_`, backticks and any dash before the comparison (Review A, round 3 Consider 2; the tail of heading parsing, low likelihood).
- Every reparse point counts as a link on Windows (one `os.lstat` rule on every Python since round 5): OneDrive placeholders and deduplicated files under `types/` or `traits/` fail as `form`. Fail-closed by design; a workflow folder is not expected in a synced folder (Review A, round 3 Consider 3, settled for version dependence by R5-2).
- Container blocks are not modelled: a fence opened inside a list item stays open for the check after Markdown ends the item, a heading inside a block quote or list item (`> ## Set at x`, `- ## Set at x`) is not a heading candidate, and a fence line inside any HTML block (types 1 to 7: `<pre>`, `<div>`, `<!-- -->`) is read as a fence; each can hide a change heading Markdown displays. A fail-closed rule (a container marker before `##`, or an indented fence followed by a less-indented line, as `form`) would close most of it (the security auditor rounds 4 and 5, Review A round 5 Consider 2; converged, and both graded it for recording; the tail of heading parsing).
- Line-break characters Markdown does not end a line at (vertical tab, form feed, `\x1c` to `\x1e`, NEL, U+2028, U+2029) are now inside a line for the check, as for Markdown, so `prose<FF>## Set at x` or `note<FF>Workflows: …` is not read, where a raw-text reader would see a line; reporting any such character in a types/, traits/ or status file as `form` would close it both ways (Review A, round 6 Consider).
- Table cells are trimmed with `str.strip()` (any Unicode whitespace); Markdown trims spaces and tabs only, so a separator followed by a form feed, NEL, U+2028 or a no-break space is accepted where Markdown shows no table; trim `" \t"` only (the security auditor, round 6).
- An escaped pipe (`\|`) inside a cell is split as a cell boundary; the tables' own rule says no cell contains a pipe, which the check does not enforce (the security auditor, round 6).

## Stage B2 — TASK-008, the Workflows line enforced
- conformance-check.py reads an existing but unreadable docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md as empty text, so its Workflows line row says "missing" where "not checked" would be exact; the row is MISSING either way and the verdict is the same (the builder's assumption, accepted at the accept commit).
- `judge_workflows_line` is written twice, identically, in inventory-check.py and conformance-check.py; a lead for stage B2's refactoring pass (one shared helper beside workflow-check.py). Closed at the pass, kept on purpose (refactor-scout, 2026-10-17; Review A of the pass): of its roughly forty code lines, about fourteen load workflow-check.py by path with no bytecode and fail closed, and the rest judge the line (read the rules, load_tables, check_status, check_placement, the outcome). A new shared file would need that same guarded loader in each program to load it, for about ten lines saved, against a new installed file, its manifest entry and three new not-loadable cases per program. Moving the judging into workflow-check.py itself, which both programs already load and the manifest lists, has none of those costs, but it changes a tested failure: with escape_field missing from the checker, the not-loadable cause would read NameError where it reads AttributeError today (tests/test_workflows_line_check.py, no-escape-field), and a refactoring pass carries no behaviour change. A lead for a later change to these programs: the judging in workflow-check.py, the two programs keeping the loader and one call, the case's expected cause changed with it; until then a change to one copy is made to both, or the divergence is recorded as intended. TASK-024's Scope holds conformance-check.py but not inventory-check.py, and it reports a line naming an unwritten workflow, findings both copies drop today: its brief either widens its Scope to inventory-check.py where the judge changes, or records the conformance copy's divergence as intended (Review A, round 2 of the refactoring pass). The pass's second candidate, one `load_rules` function in workflow-check.py for the rule-reading block written in main, both judges and a test helper (about ten net lines), was dropped: its payoff is the place a third rule file would be added, a reuse that no planned increment needs.
- Names and reasons echoed from a status file can carry text such as `RESULT: OK` or `RESULT: CONFORMANT` inside one output line (`WORKFLOWS LINE: malformed: RESULT: OK - not a trait name`); no line can be forged, since every field is escaped. Readers of either program take the verdict from the exit code or the last line, never from a search of the whole output (the security auditor, round 1).
- `Workflows: not yet classified` passes both programs at any phase; nothing forces classification after intake. By design: intake classifies a new project, calibration adds the line to a running one (TASK-023); a check that the line is classified past intake would close it (the security auditor, round 1, out of lane).
- Loading the checker writes no bytecode since round 2, but a `__pycache__/workflow_check.*.pyc` already in the bridge folder would still be read in place of the source the manifest verifies; the same class as the bridge's other module loads, and it needs write access to the bridge folder (the security auditor, round 1).

## Stage B2 — BUG-003, round 2's fixes
- workflow-check.py's `escape_field` keeps a literal backslash, so a name that contains the four characters backslash, x, e, 9 prints exactly like a name holding é (escaped as backslash-x-e-9), and the text backslash, x, 0, a like an escaped line feed; no line or RESULT can be forged (the field never holds a raw line break). Escaping the backslash itself would depart from the agreed escape forms (those of Python's backslashreplace) and change what inventory-check and conformance-check print; no current fixture holds a literal backslash, so no exact-output entry would change (the entries' backslashes are escapes, such as form-part-arrow.md's arrow). A lead for a later change to the check's output (security auditor, BUG-003, advisory; the cost restated at round 3).
- `collect` records a layer path that is not a folder with the kind `link`, which gives the required line today; recording it as `file` gives byte-identical output and keeps the label true if link handling ever gets a reason of its own, and the docstring would then name it (Review A, BUG-003, consider).

## Stage B2 — the stage review (round 5, 2026-10-17)

**Taken on BUG-006 (the supervisor's fixtures, tests and registry text, no program change).** Round 5 rejected for the class round 4 had: rules of the workflow check that no test fails for. Rather than wait for each reviewer to find one, the supervisor ran an operator sweep of workflow-check.py: run/supervisor/sweep_workflow_check.py, local, temporary copies only. It made 425 mutants, of which 381 compile, and found 89 of those surviving the workflow-check and Workflows-line tests before that round's cases were written (36 after them, listed below). Every reviewer finding and each sweep survivor that a test can separate now has its case, with exact output.

**Fences and headings:**
- a closing run indented four spaces is content (fixtures 1, blocking);
- two backticks and two tildes open no fence;
- a heading indented four spaces and an empty `## ` heading are prose.

**Change words** (fixtures 1):
- each change word without `at`;
- each stripped mark, and two marks together;
- a two-word heading ending `at`, and `AT` in capitals.

**Set values:**
- a Set value right under its heading, with spaces around it and prose after it, is its first non-blank line, stripped;
- a backtick on one side only is not unwrapped.

**Files and folders:**
- a sub-workflow file whose key is no sub-type (tests, blocking);
- two different modules replaced in one file;
- a folder under `traits/`;
- a folder inside an unknown type folder;
- an unlisted kind named on another point (fixtures 2).

**Workflows lines:**
- a rest only starting as `not yet classified`, or differing from it by case or a space, is read as names (tests and fixtures 4, converged);
- an indented or lower-case `Workflows:` line is not one (fixtures 3);
- trait order and repeats beyond neighbouring traits (fixtures 3);
- a product type's Name, a sub-type's Name in another case, and a traits text with a space before it or `None` (fixtures 4);
- a status file with no final line feed;
- each head separator case's whole output.

**The two rule files' table reader:**
- a table right under its heading;
- alignment colons;
- rows without a trailing pipe;
- a table with no row;
- the heading given twice;
- a prose line ending in a pipe;
- a section with no table.

**Output escaping:** the escape at each width's boundary.

**Mutation drivers:** run/supervisor/mutate_workflow_check.py names a mutant for each of these, 151 of 151 killed at this round (161 of 161 after BUG-006's gate rounds below: 159 at r2, 160 at r3, 161 at r4), and mutate_workflows_line.py stands at 42 of 42.

**Registry text** (texts part; Review B's should-fix): the registry now states that a section runs to the next `##` heading, so a replacement's own structure uses `###`, and (since BUG-006 r4) what a `##` heading is: at most three spaces, `##`, then a space or a tab, so a line of `##` alone is text. It states the fence rules in full (indent, info string, closing run) and what else fails:
- an unclosed fence;
- an empty Set;
- a non-workflow file under `types/` or `traits/`;
- an unknown type folder.

It also says workflow-check.py's `DELIVERY_KINDS` changes with the list. The delivery-kinds test reads every kind the list names however it is described. test_open_list holds that no name is `none` or `not yet classified` (tests part).

The class has now been found in rounds 5, 6 and 7 each time with operators the earlier fixes did not try: ISS-007. BUG-008 takes every item and widens the sweep to those operators.

**Blocking, taken in BUG-008 (the supervisor's tests and fixtures; no program change):**
- **Tests:** five mutants of the judges' output passed (the malformed row's first finding only, joined by `, `; the not-checked row's and lines' first cause only; a registry error ignored). New: test_r015_conformance_prints_each_finding_in_order (the whole row), a `registry-absent` case, and every not-loadable case asserting each cause whole, in both programs, from the copy's own reader (`_causes`); the open list without tables gives three causes, its three missing headings, each named. mutate_workflows_line.py has the mutants (55 of 55).
- **Fixtures 2:** the four forms loosened by a regex literal passed (a trailing space on three forms, two spaces between words, `re.I` on Set and Replace). form-heading-trailing-text holds nineteen loosened headings, one per way and form: two spaces after `##` (the widened sweep's own find), two spaces between words, a space after the key or the kind, lower case; its output is 24 lines, each pinned; the headings that end in a space are held by a byte test.
- **Fixtures 4 and 5:** `none` beside a trait (form-traits-none-first, -none-last), an unprefixed `not yet classified` among several lines (form-prefix-missing-unclassified), and a space or words before `not yet classified` (form-unclassified-leading-space, -ending).

**The widened sweep's own survivors, killed:** a `+` straight after `##` read as a heading (`##+Add at ...` in form-heading-trailing-text, no finding); a `+` after a closing run read as closing (the pass root's ```` ```+ ```` inside a fence); a second word starting or ending with `at` read as `at` (the pass root's `## Look atop the target`, `## Go combat it`); only the first separator cell checked (test_r014_separator_cell_count_differs[second-cell-not-dashes]); only the first ERROR printed (test_r014_every_unreadable_file_is_reported); a name ending `md` without the dot read as `.md` (form-not-markdown's installablemd, notesmd, host-extensionmd, fixtures 2's consider). mutate_workflow_check.py has a mutant for each (220 of 220).

**Considers, taken:** supervisor.md's grammar says each trait at most once, and the glossary's Workflow check entry says the judges use its grammar and placement checks (texts); the usage line is asserted on standard error, and the top-level links' output is compared whole (tests); form-change-word-punctuation's ten headings and fence-info-string-not-closing's inner run are asserted (fixtures 1); the pass root's README sentence is asserted (fixtures 3); a comment on STATUS_EXACT says why the single-line status files have no Phase line and why two `form-` names report unknown names (fixtures 5).

**Considers, recorded, not taken:**
- **Programs:** a folder that cannot be listed reaches the ERROR lines in os.walk's order, the filesystem's, so two such folders print in a different order on CI's two legs; the program's promise of a fixed order covers finding lines only. Sorting the errors before printing would close it, for the next change of the program.
- **Tests:** tests/test_workflow_check.py's test_r014_set_changes_cell_is_split_on_commas_and_stripped pins the registry's whole intake.target-environment row, its Phase cell included; TASK-010's rename of the registry's Phase column widens its Scope to this file too, beside tests/test_extension_points.py (the routing at line 41 of this file).
- **Fixtures 2:** the FILE_EXACT cases run against the bridge's live open list and registry; a rename there fails loudly, never silently, so they stay.
- **Fixtures 5:** the two `form-` names that report unknown names are not renamed; the comment says why.

**The widened sweep:**
<!-- widened sweep: begin -->
The widened sweep (run/supervisor/sweep_wide.py, local) made 571 compiling mutants over workflow-check.py (its new operators only) and the two judges (every operator): 395 survived before r2's cases (run/supervisor/sweep_wide_r3.out, BUG-008's first cases in) and 348 after r3's (run/supervisor/sweep_wide_r5.out), each in one class below; the generator run/supervisor/write_wide_list.py stops on a survivor no class names. The classes are the supervisor's reasoning, not a test:
- **280, token:** a comparison widened (to startswith, endswith, in, stripped or casefold) against a value from a closed set the program writes itself, none of which starts, ends or contains another or differs from another by case or a space: a walked path's kind (`dir`, `link`, `file`), a change's kind (`add`, `set`, `replace`), an item's action (`d-folder`, `form-path`, `type`, `sub`, `trait`), a layer (`type`, `sub`, `trait`), a finding code, a judge's outcome (`valid`, `missing`, `malformed`, `not-checked`), a fence marker's character, and `os.name`, which the interpreter sets to one of its registered names (`posix`, `nt`, `java`); and a walked path's first part, which is `types` or `traits`, the only two folders the walk enters (lines 302 to 338). Only a comparison whose right side is a literal, or the fence's character, is in this class (gate round 2: a key from the file was not).
- **2, point:** a heading's point key compared stripped with a delivery point: the key is `(\S+)` of the heading's pattern, so it holds no white space to strip.
- **18, no-line:** the judges' test for the no-line finding: its reason is the program's own text, no other reason starts, ends or contains it, is a part of it, or differs from it by case or a space, and check_status returns that finding alone, so the first finding is the only one.
- **24, blank:** a comparison with the empty string, or of a name with itself stripped, widened where nothing separates the two: only the empty string starts, ends or is contained in the empty string; a string differs from itself stripped exactly when stripping changes it, in any case; a last line of spaces only is blank to every reader (103); a part name or a trait name is refused when it differs from itself stripped, in the same condition (597, 651); a head name is stripped before the test (610).
- **2, words:** the change heading's second word is split on white space, so it carries none to strip, and is casefolded already.
- **6, cells:** a table cell compared stripped: the reader strips every cell it splits (line 195), so a cell has nothing to strip.
- **4, head:** the Workflows head compared to the exact head widened: the head's names are found in the open list by their exact text, so a head holding the type and its sub-type that is a part of the exact head, or equal to it in another case, holds the same names in the same order and is the exact head.
- **6, regex:** a pattern that ends `(.*)$` or `[ \t]*$` already takes trailing spaces and tabs, and holds no letter for `re.I` to change (the heading candidate, the fence's opening and closing runs).
- **4, loader:** spec_from_file_location on a path ending `.py` always returns a spec with a loader, so the judges' guard is unreachable.
- **2, printing:** inventory-check.py prints one line per row and nothing for no row, so a valid line read as malformed with no rows (228), and the one outcome left after the others, valid with no rows, read as not checked (263), print the same.
<!-- widened sweep: end -->

## Stage B2 — the stage review (round 9, 2026-10-18)

Snapshot STAGE-B2i (6e3d90f on stage/B2, deleted after verify OK, hash 53d3bbbd7b53787c). **Review B:** REJECT, two items: workflow-check.py's usage error with a standard stream closed exits 1, not 2, and with standard output closed prints a traceback (a program defect, BUG-010's fix); four status fixtures' text was not pinned (form-prefix-missing-unclassified, form-part-colon-no-space, form-traits-none-last, form-traits-marker-twice). **Review A** in nine parts: programs, texts, fixtures 1, 2, 3, 5 and 6 APPROVE with should-fix items or considers; fixtures 4 REJECT (the rule that a line breaking the prefix rule is not checked further was unobserved: every such fixture's body was valid); tests REJECT (`continue` made `break` at lines 687, 693 and 696, and the not-yet-classified test moved above the part count, each turning a failing status file into `RESULT: OK`; should-fix: `continue` made `break` at 612, 653, 657 and 660). Fixtures 1's should-fix and the texts part's consider name the same line, extension-points.md:72: blocking by convergence. ISS-007 recurs (round 9), in a new operator class: the order of statements and the way a loop is left, which no sweep had tried.

**The supervisor's response:** rather than cases for the lines named, a sweep for the class, written and run before any case (run/supervisor/sweep_flow.py: every `continue` made `break` or `pass`, every bare `return` made `pass`, every two neighbouring statements swapped, every `for` loop reversed, every member of a module constant dropped, over sweep_wide.py's ranges): 145 survivors of 444 on main's tests (run/supervisor/sweep_flow_r1.out). Every survivor an input separates in a fixed order then got a case, and every status fixture's text is pinned.

**Blocking, taken in BUG-010:**
- **Review B, the program:** CheckArgumentParser.error wrote to standard error and flushed standard output without allowing either to be `None`, Python's state for a stream the program was started without (docs/debug/BUG-010.md). The builder's fix guards the two calls (handoff/BUG-010.md, handoff/BUG-010-r1-report.md). Reproduction: test_r014_usage_error_with_a_closed_stream_exits_2_without_traceback[stdout-closed, stderr-closed], red at the checkpoint, green after the fix.
- **Review B, the fixtures:** STATUS_TEXT holds every status fixture's text; test_r014_every_status_case_holds_exactly_its_lines compares each.
- **Fixtures 4:** fixtures whose bad-prefix or no-marker line has a body that would fail, with a precondition that runs each body alone (test_r014_a_line_that_breaks_the_prefix_rule_is_not_checked_further, four cases), and a line after each that is still checked (form-bad-prefix-then-checked, form-no-traits-marker-then-checked, e-after-unclassified).
