Its consider, taken: the reproduction test asserts each open stream whole, white space aside. Noted, not taken: with both streams closed the run relies on argparse's own tolerance of a missing stream (Python 3.12, which CI pins; probed: exit 2).

**The control-flow sweep:** BUG-010's first cases killed 10 of the 145 (run/supervisor/sweep_flow_r2.out); 19 more cases (17 in tests/test_workflow_check.py, two in tests/test_workflows_line_check.py; each docstring names its survivor and cites the document that fixes its order, or says that none does) kill 22 more (run/supervisor/sweep_flow_r3.out, and line 740's by the escape case): the order of a file's findings (an open fence, a trait's empty Set at, a delivery kind at an unknown point, a sub-workflow's two identity findings) and of a status line's (two unknown segments, no type and two sub-types, the shape before the written test); the separator before the columns, and the first missing column; the first row of a repeated name and a trait's first place; both layers that cannot be examined, in order; a listing error before a read error; the usage line's option order; a root that is no folder when the rules cannot be read; a reason that holds a key, escaped; file_lines' final line feed; and the judges' load errors before a line is judged. Each pins the output as the program gives it, its order included; where no document fixes an order, a change of it is a change of these tests. On the fixed program, with every case, the full sweep leaves 113 survivors of 444 (run/supervisor/sweep_flow_r4.out); the generator run/supervisor/write_flow_list.py (`--check`) stops on a survivor no class names and on a class entry no survivor matches. The classes are the supervisor's reasoning, not a test:
- **68, independent:** two neighbouring statements swapped where neither reads what the other binds or writes: an assignment, a nested definition or a pure read beside another, an append to a list the other does not touch (261, 331), and the positional argument declared before the last option, where argparse formats and parses positionals apart from options (772).
- **15, before-exit:** a pure computation or an empty list moved above the test that returns or continues before it: nothing it binds is read on the way out, and on the way on it is bound as before.
- **6, exclusive:** two tests swapped that no value passes both, each ending in a return or continue, or ending alike: a line that starts with `|` or with `## `, an action that is one of two names, a kind that is `link` or `dir`; a link or a path that is no folder, both reported as a link; a link by mode bits or by reparse attribute, both a link.
- **6, commuting:** two steps that give one result in either order: dropping one leading and one trailing `:` (or `|`), which differ only on the one-character string, where both give the empty string; raising the running maximum and testing against it, where an index that raises it is not below it; a row appended before a test that then returns an error; a part counted where a bad prefix gives no part; a repeat test before the unknown-name test, where an unknown name never enters the seen set.
- **4, unreachable:** a guard no input reaches, moved: is_change_heading is called only on a line its own pattern matched, so its match is never None (382); a sub-workflow is walked only under a known type folder, so its type key is always a product type and the finding that used the list before it was made never runs (547); spec_from_file_location on a `.py` path always has a loader (the judges' 205, 314).
- **4, sorted:** an order the program then discards: a folder's file names and the found list, whose items are sorted by their unique path and whose unknown type folders are a set (333, 340, 345); the two streams, each reconfigured alone (834).
- **1, listing-order:** a folder's subfolders walked in reverse (325): the walk then lists them in the other order, so two subfolders that cannot be listed give their ERROR lines in the other order, an input separates it; but the original order is the file system's own listing order, which no document fixes and which differs between file systems (NTFS lists by name, ext4 by hash), so no test can pin it without fixing an order the program does not choose (BUG-010's gate round 1: Review A).
- **2, fence-line:** a fence's opening or closing line no longer skipped: such a line starts with backticks or tildes after at most three spaces, so the heading candidate never matches it.
- **1, error-stops:** the walk goes on past a layer that cannot be examined: its error is already kept, and any error stops the check before a finding is judged, so the extra link item is never printed.
- **1, same-list:** the list of a root's items appended before its items are read: the same list object, filled before it is read.
- **2, placement-empty:** the placement findings added before the missing-line test: a status file with no Workflows line has no placement finding (check_placement returns none), so the missing outcome is unchanged.
- **3, streams:** the order of BUG-010's fixed usage error: the RESULT line printed before the error line, which go to different streams, so each stream, read on its own, receives the same text (merged into one, `2>&1` with standard output unbuffered, the lines could change places, an order no document governs); the flush of standard output moved above the print, or the exit above the flush, where sys.exit raises SystemExit and the interpreter flushes standard output as it ends.

The earlier sweeps rerun on the fixed program: the first sweep 49 survivors of 758 (run/supervisor/sweep_workflow_check_r14.out), r13's less line 103's empty-last-line test forced false (the final-line-feed case kills it) and with the fixed method's flush guard forced false (the interpreter flushes standard output as it ends); write_survivor_list.py --check holds each against its reason. The widened sweep 351 of 731, the same survivors as r8 at their new line numbers (run/supervisor/sweep_wide_r9.out), round 8's class list unchanged. The mutation drivers rerun on the fixed program: mutate_workflow_check.py 300 of 300 killed (run/supervisor/mutate_workflow_check_bug010.out; the mutant "usage error without RESULT" now drops the guarded flush with the RESULT line), mutate_workflows_line.py 62 of 62 (run/supervisor/mutate_workflows_line_bug010.out).

**BUG-010's gate round 1:** Review A APPROVE, with two should-fix items and notes, taken in r2 (tests, texts and records; no program change). Review B did not run: its model's pool was used up (ISS-008). On the owner's word the native profile runs; since its Review B is of the family that built r1's fix, the fix was authored again by composer-2.5 (handoff/BUG-010.md, run r2; handoff/BUG-010-r2-report.md: the method restored, the reproduction tests red at 1453 passed and 2 failed, the fix implemented, 1455 passed, its three mutants caught). Its text is r1's, two guards, byte for byte; Review B of gate round 2 is grok-4.7-medium.
- **Should-fix, taken:** line 325's survivor (a folder's subfolders walked in reverse) was filed under "sorted", whose reason does not hold for it: the walk lists the subfolders in the other order, so two that cannot be listed give their ERROR lines in the other order. That order is the file system's own, which no document fixes and which differs between file systems, so 325 has a class of its own, "listing-order", and the claim reads "every survivor an input separates in a fixed order". The order tests' docstrings now cite the document that fixes an order (TASK-006.md Requirement 11 for a status line's findings) or say that none does (the layers, the listing and read errors), say where an input breaks the open list's own rules (a name given twice, a key with a letter outside its rule), and attribute only the reporting itself to R5-1.

## Stage B2 — the stage review (round 11, 2026-10-18)

Snapshot STAGE-B2k (074a378 on stage/B2, deleted after verify OK, hash 8603c48974e9f62d). **Review B** (grok-4.7-medium, the native profile): REJECT. The delivery-kind membership tests (527, 530, 536) made on the kind case-folded survived every test, and accepted `Installer`. The guard's `verify` found workflow-check.py still mutated by the reviewer, though its reply said it had restored the file; the guard's restore put it back, and nothing reached the branch. **Review A** in nine parts: tests and fixtures 3 REJECT, the other seven APPROVE with considers. The tests part's blocking item: the placement's prefix tests (717, 722, 729) made on the line after `lstrip()` or `lower()`. Its should-fix: allows_set split on `", "`, and on the cell stripped and lowered; the separator's colons removed with `lstrip(':')`, and its dashes tested with `"-" in text`; the table reader (210, 219) and the heading candidate (462) after `lstrip()`; the head split on `", "` (605). Its considers: 388's `casefold` made `lower` (a long s), 486's slice made a strip, 420's info string read as its first word, the tab variants of `strip(" ")` (285, 195, 484), and inventory-check.py 197's `realpath` made `abspath`. Fixtures 3's blocking item: a type file keyed by a sub-type's or a trait's key, and a trait file keyed by a product type's (566 and 568 reading another table's key). Its should-fix: normalize_kind unwrapping before it strips, or stripping again after. ISS-007 recurs (round 11), in a new operator class: a rule rewritten with another string method.

**The supervisor's response:** as in rounds 9 and 10, a sweep for the class before any case (run/supervisor/sweep_strings.py). It swaps strip, lstrip and rstrip, drops them, changes their argument or lowers after them; swaps lower, casefold and upper; changes a split's separator; normalizes a startswith or endswith receiver, or makes the test `in`; swaps match, fullmatch and search, or strips their argument; swaps realpath and abspath, and makes isfile, isdir and lexists `exists`; normalizes one side of `==` or `!=`; makes `set(x) == {c}` `c in x` or `<=`; reads a constant member's container as its first word or lowered; strips, lowers or case-folds a non-constant member; strips a slice; and makes `if X.startswith(C): X = X[1:]` `X.lstrip(C)`. It left 292 survivors of 433 on main's tests (run/supervisor/sweep_strings_main.out, one mutant not compiling) and 261 on the round's named cases (run/supervisor/sweep_strings_r1.out).

A search for separating inputs followed (run/supervisor/separate_strings.py). Each surviving mutant of workflow-check.py was loaded in-process beside the program and run on a corpus of 9,230 inputs: the status fixtures and every one-line variant of them, the open lists and the registry with each line or cell rewritten, and every fixture root with each heading, fence, Set value and first line rewritten. The search separated 17 distinct mutants (run/supervisor/separate_strings_r1.out holds three runs appended); the supervisor's reading of the rest separated more. Every survivor an input separates got a case (BUG-013, tests and fixtures only, no program change).

**The named cases:**
- unknown-type-file gains types/claude-code-extension.md and types/installable.md, and unknown-trait-file gains traits/web-app.md, each pinned in FILE_EXACT.
- test_r014_set_changes_cell_is_split_on_commas_and_stripped gains `add,set`, `add,<tab>set` and `add, Set`.
- test_r014_separator_cell_count_differs gains `::---`, `-x-` and `--:--`.
- Further tests: test_r014_a_delivery_kind_is_matched_exactly, test_r014_a_delivery_kind_value_is_stripped_then_unwrapped, test_r014_an_indented_heading_line_stays_in_its_section, test_r014_the_change_word_is_case_folded, test_r014_a_backtick_anywhere_in_the_info_opens_no_fence, test_r014_a_head_split_on_a_comma_without_its_space, test_r014_an_indented_table_line_is_no_table_line, test_r014_a_header_cell_is_stripped_of_tabs and test_r015_placement_reads_the_prefixes_exactly.
- The pass root's host-installation precondition (fixtures 3, consider).

**The sweep's cases:**
- **Table reader:** the separator cells `: ---`, `--- :` and `---::` (172, 174, 173); `-:` and `:-` (171); a header line ending in a tab (190); `## ` inside a line or after an indent, and a bare `## ` before the table (213).
- **Walk:** file names matched exactly, `notes.md.txt`, `web-app.MD` and a space before `.md` (360 to 365, 566, 568, 805); a name ending in a space after `.md` (360, 362, 364, where the file system keeps it); type folders `Host-Extension` and ` host-extension` (342, 347); an open list keyed in capitals (547, 566, 568).
- **Headings:** a Set or Replace heading repeated in another case (507, 513); `for Installer` on another point (530); a bare `## ` ending a section (462).

## Hotfix 2026.10.18a — the design critique's ceiling (HOTFIX-001, merged on release/2026.10.11 as 4624421, PR #39; carried onto main 2026-10-19)

**Leads (the code gate, three rounds; recorded, not taken):**
- **The trim's time on long runs.** `DESIGN_TRIM_BEFORE_SEP` (`[. ]+(?=[/\\])`) is tried at every start of a run of dots or spaces, so a prompt holding one run of many thousand dots or spaces before a separator takes time quadratic in its length. A launch prompt is the constant pointer, so no real prompt comes near it. Check: time `design_ceiling` on a prompt of 100,000 dots followed by `/`; if it takes more than a second, match the run once (a possessive or atomic form, or a hand-written scan).
- **An allowed name followed by a space and a separator.** `run/review/DESIGN-ceiling-3.md /x` is read with the trim and refused as a name that cannot be read, where the file is an allowed round. The refusal fails closed and no launch is written that way. Check: a test with that argument, and the token's end taken at the first space before the trim runs.
- **Accepted limits of the launcher's reading.** A path through `x/..`, a Windows 8.3 short name of the folder, a prompt that names the file in other words, and a second launch of an allowed round file are not refused; the text forbids each and KP-062 names the sign. Check: none planned; the record of each round's commit is the audit.
- **The scope check's base on later rounds.** `scope-check.py` measures from the brief's first commit, so the supervisor's own commits between rounds (a round-2 brief, round-file commits) show as SCOPE DRIFT on round 2 and after; the builder's own change was confirmed with `git diff --stat <the round's brief commit>`. Check: a `--base` for re-delegations (a bridge feedback lead).
- **The builder's baselines.** The builder's own suite runs showed the install and rollback tests failing in its environment only, where the supervisor's `test-runner` runs were green (944 passed, 20 skipped). Check: run the rollback tests under the agent home with the launcher's stripped environment once, to learn which variable they read (a bridge feedback lead).

## Hotfix 2026.10.25a — the report cycle's program follow-ups (HOTFIX-002, merged on release/2026.10.11 as c0822c0, PR #83; carried onto main 2026-10-25)

**The carry.** The release branch's change against 4624421 applied three-way onto origin/main; three files conflicted. tests/conftest.py: main's FIX-001 plus the hotfix's `BRIDGE_REVIEW_HASHES` temporary folder, the result equal to c0822c0's file. conformance-check.py: main's `Workflows:` line check (R-015) and the hotfix's computer-path check both kept, in that order, the docstring naming both. MANIFEST.json: main's taken, then stamped 2026.10.25a by make-manifest.py over main's tree with VERSION (which did not conflict: main had not changed it since 4624421). Fitting it to main's release B code took three more changes the release branch never met: records-check.py and stage-diff.py gained the top-level build_parser() main's command reference requires (CFG-B3b; the builder, handoff/HOTFIX-002.md, the move alone against each c0822c0 file), and conformance-check.py's docstring names its path patterns in words, since the reference copies it whole and refuses a drive-letter path (round 2); seven test launches drop an explicit `creationflags=NO_WINDOW` for FIX-001's scan, the computer-path test counts the line from main's workspace() status file, and test_dump_cli.py lists the two programs among the argparse ones.

**Leads (recorded, not taken):**
- **Computer paths in the maintainer's tools.** evaluate-reports.py's project table gains the bridge's own root beside the three it already held on main, and test_hotfix25a_loopguard_reports.py defaults `TOKEN_USAGE_PY` to the maintainer's script by its drive path (skipped where absent). Rule 54 keeps computer paths out of the supervisor's commits; these are the maintainer's tool configuration, carried as built. Check: the table and the default read from a file outside the repository, at release T. **Narrower than rule 54 (stage B5's review, round 1, records-hardening S6):** rule 54 keeps every computer path out of a commit, whoever's tool carries it; BUG-031 drops the test's default, so TOKEN_USAGE_PY alone names the script; evaluate-reports.py's project table, a program, stays this lead, its check as above, and with it tests/test_hotfix25a_loopguard_reports.py's `test_c1_the_bridge_project_is_listed_and_read_from_its_workspace_key_only`, which asserts the table's drive path and its encoded project keys (`E--p18-...`, a project folder's name, no user name; stage B5's review, round 2, records-hardening C).
- **The core's `Layout:` template is the absolute form.** bridge/commands/supervisor.md's status template gives `Layout: Workspace=<resolved path>  Documents=<resolved path>`, which the carried conformance check marks MISSING; only the change log's Running projects must says to name the folders relative to the project root. Check: the template reads `Workspace/` and `Documents/`, at release T (text) (Review A, HOTFIX-002).
- **records-check.py's ID pattern has no left boundary.** ID_RX reads `HOTFIX-002` inside a parenthesised trailer as `FIX-002` (Review A simulated "chore: x (HOTFIX-002) (#84)"), and the CHANGES.md lookup would then miss it; main's subjects put the HOTFIX id first and parse correctly today. Check: a `(?<![A-Za-z0-9])` before the ID and a test with that subject, in the hotfix's next program change (Review A, HOTFIX-002).

### TASK-028 (round 1's reviews; recorded, not taken)
- **Rounds 4 and 5 ran past the owner's three-round ceiling (stage B5's review, round 1, records-hardening S3):** round 4 for round 3's two defects, a NameError in uv_run_pytest (behaviour against the brief) and a testpaths entry holding `..` that read a conftest.py outside the project (security); round 5 for Review A's round-4 findings graded by the five (run/supervisor/task028-reviewA-r4.md), a traceback printing the program's and the Workspace's absolute paths and an OK row printing a control character (each behaviour against the brief's Requirement 9, rule 54); every other finding of rounds 2 to 5 is recorded in this section.
- **The interpreter's name is canonicalised loosely.** bridge-run.py's head_of strips `.exe` and `.cmd` wherever they stand in the name, so `pyt.exehon` and `python.cmd` pass the interpreter test, and the command's resolution may then run a `.cmd` or `.bat` through cmd.exe; the test kind shares the decision (python_interpreter_ok), so a fix changes the test kind's behaviour too. Check: a test per form (a suffix inside the name, a `.cmd` beside a `.exe` on PATH) for both kinds, then the suffix stripped only at the end and a resolved `.cmd`/`.bat` refused, in an increment whose Scope takes the test kind (the security audit, round 1, advisory; pre-existing). **Half taken by BUG-029** (dbeb2e2; stage B5's review, round 1, records-hardening S1, briefs-launcher C3): head_of strips `.exe` or `.cmd` only at the name's end, so `pyt.exehon` is refused; the other half stays open: `python.cmd` still passes python_interpreter_ok, and the resolution may run a `.cmd` or `.bat` through cmd.exe.
- **The allow rule matches a second `--`.** settings.bridge.json's rules `... --kind driver -- *` and `... --kind test -- *` also match `--limit auto -- <anything> --kind driver -- x`; the launcher parses the first `--`, so accepted() decides and nothing unchecked runs, but the rule admits a command line it was not written for. Check: a test that the launcher refuses every such line for both kinds, then a narrower rule if Claude Code's matcher allows one (the security audit, round 1, advisory; pre-existing).
- **A `.venv` interpreter reached through a link is not checked.** A path-qualified interpreter under the launch folder's `.venv` is accepted for the test and driver kinds without a link walk, so a junction inside `.venv` can point the interpreter anywhere. Check: a junction test for both kinds, then the same walk the driver's program gets, in an increment whose Scope takes the test kind (Review A, round 1, noted; pre-existing).
- **A driver run is healthy at exit 1, whatever ended it.** The driver kind counts exit 1 as healthy, the mutation set having completed with survivors, but a driver that crashes with a traceback also exits 1, so a crash is logged healthy and teaches the ceiling; it cannot defeat the ceiling (a hang ends at 124, a crash is short, the cap holds). Check: TASK-031's brief requires mutation-run.py to catch everything at its top level and exit 2 or more on an internal error, with a test, and the supervisor's own drivers under run/supervisor/ follow the same contract (Review A, round 1, consider).
- **The window after the re-check.** Between the driver's re-check, `subprocess.Popen` and the interpreter opening the script lie tens of milliseconds in which a folder on the program's path can be swapped for a junction; no path check closes it, and exploiting it needs what the audit's precondition already grants (writing under run/supervisor/). Check: if the precondition ever changes, open the program once and hand the interpreter the open file (or its content) instead of the path (the security audit, round 2, advisory 8).
- **tox.ini is never read by the windowless floor.** pytest reads `tox.ini`'s `[pytest]` before `setup.cfg`; the floor skips `tox.ini` (round 1's brief), so a project with both is judged by `setup.cfg`. Check: a fixture with both files, then `tox.ini` read in pytest's place, at the next change to the floor (Review A, round 2, noted).
- **The driver's leading-option refusal is redundant.** A token starting with `-` can never end in `.py` inside run/supervisor/ or equal the mutation path, so the explicit refusal can be removed with no test failing; it is kept as a plain statement of the rule. Check: none needed; drop it only in a refactoring pass that keeps a test of the refused forms (Review A, round 2, noted).
- **RecursionError at the other JSON floors.** conformance-check.py's project.json and settings-JSON reads catch ValueError but not RecursionError, so a file nested thousands deep prints a traceback (with the installed program's path) and ends the check; the windowless floor catches it since round 3. Check: one test each, then RecursionError added, in the next increment that edits those floors (the security audit, round 2, advisory 3).
- **conformance-check.py loads bridge_env.py with a second copy of is_link beside it.** `_load_bridge_env` (round 3) falls back to an inline is_link when bridge_env.py is not beside the program (tests/test_workflows_line_check.py's `_bridge_copy` copies conformance-check.py alone), adds `sys.path.insert(0, HERE)` with no import after it, and execs bridge_env.py without `sys.dont_write_bytecode`, so a run beside bridge_env.py leaves a .pyc (the docstring says the check never writes). No security effect: the installed tree always holds bridge_env.py, and both callers lstat first and fail closed. Check: a plain `import bridge_env` (or the spec load under dont_write_bytecode), the fallback deleted, and the copying tests copy bridge_env.py too, in one increment whose Scope takes those tests (Review A, round 3, H1; the security audit, round 3, 3; both graded it outside the blocking classes).
- **The runner word can still name a secret given as an option's value.** `--token S3cr3t9 mocha tests` prints `S3cr3t9`, and `ghp_...` as the first word prints the token; 9(f) skips only words holding `=` or starting with `-`. An npm script cannot start with an option, so the case is contrived. Check: skip the word after a `-` option and redact credential shapes (KP-021), with a test each (the security audit, round 3, 4; Review A, round 3, H6).
- **A hard-linked conftest.py, and the root configuration files, are read without a link check.** A root or test-root conftest.py hard-linked to an outside file gives OK (is_link does not count hard links), and pytest.ini, pyproject.toml, setup.cfg and package.json are read even when they are file links (pre-round-3). Check: st_nlink and the link test on each file the floor reads, with a test each (the security audit, round 3, 5 and 6).
- **Round 3's mutation table overstated three kills.** handoff/TASK-028-r3-report.md lists as killed the dash-skip removal, the `=` rule narrowed back to NAME=VALUE, and `normpath` at one of three sites; each survives (the `=` rule refuses `--token=abc123` anyway; shlex yields `TOKEN=abc4x def7z`, which the narrow pattern still matches; abspath is applied again later). Check: tests `-pS3cr3t9 mocha tests` and `a.b=S3cr3t9 mocha tests` naming mocha; the runner-word tests also asserting no Traceback and an exit below 2 (Review A, round 3, H2 and H5).
- **The rootdir conftest.py link rule is pinned only on Linux CI.** test_r039_root_conftest_symlink skips on the owner's computer (a file symbolic link needs a privilege), so locally the rule's mutant survives. Check: none needed while CI's ubuntu job runs it; a junction cannot stand in for a file link (the security audit, round 3, 7; Review A, round 3).
- **_pytest_test_roots carries a dead branch.** `if _test_root_path_ok(ws, "tests"): return ["tests"]` is followed by the same return; main() makes the real check. Check: remove it in the stage-close refactoring pass (Review A, round 3, H4).
- **The by-class line on the floor's input handling (the supervisor, round 4's reviews).** Rounds 2 to 4 each found new inputs that crashed the windowless floor or printed something it should not (a malformed config, deep nesting, a link before `..`, a NUL, an unlistable folder, a control character in a folder name): the long tail of one subsystem. Round 5 fixes round 4's three and adds a backstop that turns any exception in the floor into one NOTE naming its class. From then on, a further finding of this kind that fails closed (never an OK from a file outside the project, nothing read outside it) is recorded here, not re-delegated; one that gives a wrong OK, reads outside the project or prints a secret still blocks (step 7's by-class line, the owner's ceiling).
- **The uv test does not pin the ALLOW_ANY forms.** test_r039_uv_run_without_pytest_still_refused covers the test and builder kinds and no kind, refused; under BRIDGE_RUN_ALLOW_ANY=1 the same forms start as the test kind, as before TASK-028 (the security audit's round-4 comparison with 309ed38), but no test pins it. Check: one case per form with ALLOW_ANY, expecting a start (Review A, round 4, H2).
- **The POSIX link-then-`..` test is confirmed by reasoning only.** test_r039_testpaths_link_then_dotdot_posix skips on Windows; that it kills the `..`-rule mutant is reasoned (Review A, round 4). Check: the CI ubuntu job runs it on every pull request; one mutation run there when TASK-031's driver exists (Review A, round 4, H4).
- **Round 4's report read `Assumed: none`.** The fix rests on POSIX resolving `lnk/..` through the link, which the builder could not observe (the test skipped). Check: none beyond the item above; recorded as unknown, not none, in 4dedcfd's message (Review A, round 4, H3).
- **The backstop's NOTE lowers the MISSING count (round 5; named by both reviewers, so graded by its class).** When the windowless floor raises, its one NOTE takes the place of the row it would have printed, and the exit code is as for any NOTE (the round-5 brief's choice): a floor that would be MISSING counts no longer toward exit 1, so a project whose only gap is this floor would print RESULT: CONFORMANT. The security audit (HARDENING) and Review A (noted) both name it, which makes it blocking by the convergence rule; past the ceiling it sends the work back only in one of the five classes, and it is in none: no project input reaches the backstop (crafted inputs and 300,000 fuzzed configurations; only resource exhaustion, a MemoryError on a huge conftest.py), the NOTE is never an OK, and the brief, not the builder, chose the exit. Check: the backstop's NOTE counts toward the non-zero exit (or is a MISSING), with a test asserting exit 1 where it is the only gap, in the next increment whose Scope holds conformance-check.py, as TASK-031's brief has mutation-run.py exit 2 or above on an internal error (the security audit and Review A, round 5; the supervisor's grading).
- **A second link test after a successful lstat.** conformance-check.py's `_ws_walk_component_ok` wraps `bridge_env.is_link` in the same `(OSError, ValueError)` guard as the `os.lstat` before it, which cannot raise once lstat has succeeded; harmless and within round 5's requirement 1. Check: drop it in the stage-close refactoring pass, keeping test_r039_nul_in_testpaths_dropped (Review A, round 5, noted).
- **A NUL entry on POSIX is assumed to reach the link walk.** That `os.path.normpath` neither raises nor truncates on an embedded NUL off Windows was observed on Windows only; were it to raise on Linux, the backstop would turn it into its NOTE and test_r039_nul_in_testpaths_dropped would fail visibly, so it fails closed either way. Check: that test's result on the CI ubuntu job (Review A, round 5, noted).

### TASK-029 (round 1's reviews; recorded, not taken)
- **Off Windows a launcher killed alone frees the slot while its command runs.** On POSIX the launcher starts its command in a session of its own (`os.setsid`) and the slot's lock descriptor is not inheritable, so a kill of the launcher process alone releases the lock while the command's tree still runs, and a waiter starts a second heavy run beside it; on Windows the Job Object ends the tree with the launcher (reproduced: the next run took the slot 0.18 s after the kill, the child gone). The owner's computers run Windows; Linux runs only CI's tests. Check: a POSIX test killing the launcher alone and asserting the waiter does not start while the child lives, then the descriptor passed to the command (`pass_fds`) or the session's process group killed when the launcher ends, in the next increment whose Scope holds bridge-run.py (Review A, round 1, consider).

### TASK-029 (round 2's reviews; recorded, not taken)
- **An existing `~/.cursor-bridge/` keeps its mode.** `os.makedirs(mode=0o700)` sets the mode only when it creates the folder; a folder made earlier with wider bits lets another local user read the holder record (kind, run id, pid, start, limit; never a key), the lock itself being 0o600. Check: a POSIX test with the folder pre-made 0o755, then a `chmod` to 0o700 when the folder is the user's own, in the next increment whose Scope holds bridge-run.py (security audit, round 2, advisory).
- **Liveness and creation time come from two handles.** `read_holder_record` asks `process_creation_epoch` and `process_alive`, each opening the pid itself, so a pid reused between the two calls could pair one process's creation time with another's liveness; the window is microseconds and the creation-time match already refuses a reuse that lands before the first call. Check: one `OpenProcess` handle (`PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION | SYNCHRONIZE`) answering both (security audit, round 2, advisory).
- **The bound is converted to the monotonic clock with the wall time of the wait's start.** `deadline_mono = wait_mono + (bound_wall - wait_wall)` uses the wall clock read when the wait began, not when the bound is fixed, so a wall clock set between the two shifts the deadline by that step (bounded above by the provisional bound either way). Check: an in-process test jumping `time.time` between the wait's start and the first live record (Review A, round 2, consider).
- **On Linux the creation time from `/proc/<pid>/stat` and `btime` may move by a second after a clock adjustment.** The record then reads as no record and the waiter names `unknown`, its bound the provisional one: it fails safe. Check: none needed unless a Linux computer runs builders (the builder's assumption, round 2).

### TASK-029 (round 3's reviews; past the owner's three-round ceiling, recorded, not taken)
- **A builder run labelled `--kind review` takes no slot.** The review refusal turns away every command the launcher knows as another kind unless its head is `cursor-agent`, so `--kind review -- cursor-agent <a builder prompt>` runs without the slot; under `BRIDGE_RUN_ALLOW_ANY=1` a suite the launcher does not name (`python -X utf8 -m pytest`) runs as a review too. The slot is cooperative: the loop's builder command is a constant `--kind builder`, and review_family_refusal turns away a mislabelled run of the branch's last builder family. Graded by both reviewers outside the five blocking classes. Check: a test of a `--kind review` cursor-agent launch, then a flag only a review carries required under that kind (Review A, round 3, should fix 1; security audit, round 3, advisory 2).
- **Taken next, as BUG-029: `head_of` strips `.exe` and `.cmd` anywhere in a name.** `head_of` strips `.exe` and `.cmd` wherever they stand while `resolve` swaps in the real agent only for an exact name, so a file named `cursor-agent.exe.cmd` passes the allow list as the agent and runs as itself through `cmd.exe`, without the stripped environment, and without the slot when labelled `--kind review`. It predates the branch (97914f0a:120); a security finding in passing, fixed in its own increment right after TASK-029: one exact basename test for head_of, kind_of and resolve, a cursor-agent given by path refused (security audit, round 3, advisory 1).
- **A double close in publish_holder_record.** When `json.dump` raises inside `with os.fdopen(fd)`, the file object has closed the descriptor and the `except` closes it again (EBADF, caught); harmless while the launcher has no threads. Check: drop the inner close (both reviewers, round 3).
- **The record's validation sits outside the read's guard.** No raise between the open and the returned record holds by reading the validators; the broad `except` covers the read and the parse only. Check: the validation inside the same guard (security audit, round 3, advisory 5).
- **On Windows a link at the record's path is followed, and the record's descriptor stays open across the liveness checks.** No `O_NOFOLLOW` exists there, so a same-account program could point the record at a slow target (an oplock, an unreachable UNC path) and stall a waiter's open past its bound; and the open descriptor widens the window in which the holder's `os.replace` fails (it fails safe: one note, waiters say unknown). Check: an open with `FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` semantics, and the descriptor closed right after the read (security audit, advisory 6; Review A, consider 7).
- **Three test gaps.** No POSIX test plants a FIFO holding a valid record, so `S_ISREG`'s refusal is pinned only together with `O_NONBLOCK` (the supervisor added the link to a regular, valid record, which pins `O_NOFOLLOW`); the `except` branch's removal of the temporary file after a write failure is exercised by no test; `fchmod` on an existing lock with looser bits is untested (security audit, advisory 3; Review A, considers 2 and 5).

### TASK-029 (round 4's reviews; past the owner's three-round ceiling)
- **Taken in round 4: a driver's program was not checked again after the slot's wait.** `verify_driver_program_abs` ran once, before `acquire_heavy_slot`, and the command started after the wait with no second check, so a program under `run/supervisor/` replaced during the wait by a hard link to an outside file ran, and its row was marked healthy (Review B, reproduced; a defeated safety mechanism of TASK-028's, docs/INVENTORY.md's "checked again just before the start"). handoff/TASK-029-r4.md; the supervisor's test test_r040_driver_rechecked_after_the_wait.
- **The window between the second check and the start stays.** A path check followed by a start by path can always be raced; the second check narrows the window from the wait's length to the time until the child interpreter opens the script, after its process creation and Python's startup (tens to hundreds of milliseconds; the security audit, round 4). Check: start the program from an open descriptor or compare a file identity taken at the check with the one the interpreter opens (handoff/TASK-029-r4.md, Out of scope).
- **`slot taken` is printed before the record is published.** A waiter that polls in between reads the previous holder's record, dead, or none, and says `held by unknown`; at scale 1 its next waiting line comes a minute later. It failed one supervisor test on CI's Windows job once (fixed in the test, a4cb89c, and in three more tests with the same race). Check: publish, then print (Review A, round 4, Consider; taken with BUG-029, which edits the same file).
- **Refusing after the wait without releasing the slot is not caught by a test.** The launcher exits right after its 125 and the operating system frees the lock then, so a mutant that skips the release survives; the release is tidiness while the launcher ends at once. Check: an in-process test of the refusal arm (the brief packager, round 4).
- **The interpreter is checked by name only, before and after the wait.** `python_interpreter_ok` reads the name; on Windows `shutil.which` fixes the path before the wait and the file there (the project's own `.venv` interpreter included) can be replaced during it; on POSIX `python` is looked up on PATH at the start; Windows `shutil.which` searches the launch folder before PATH, so a `python.exe` planted there becomes the interpreter of the driver and test kinds (older than this branch). Check: resolve the interpreter to an absolute path outside the launch folder, or only the `.venv` one, and check its file identity again with the program (the security audit, round 4).
- **The installed mutation-run program is matched by path only.** `verify_driver_program_abs` accepts `~/.claude/cursor-bridge/mutation-run.py` by path equality, with no regular-file, link-count or link-walk test, so the second check cannot see a swap there (TASK-028's). Check: the same tests on that arm (the security audit, round 4).
- **A refusal after the wait leaves this run's holder record.** The record published when the slot was taken still names the run after the launcher exits, as on the Popen-failure arms; its pid is dead, so waiters read it as no record. Check: remove the record on those arms (the security audit and Review A, round 4).
- **The builder's round-4 report repeats its first runs' figures as the re-runs'.** The "after mutations restored" summaries are byte-identical to the first runs, durations included, and its nine errors are not explained; the supervisor's own run settles the suite (2430 passed, 38 skipped). For the stage close's Feedback, beside rounds 2's untrue mutation table (Review A, round 4).

### BUG-029 (the brief's reality check; recorded, not taken)
- **A path-qualified `gh` is accepted by its basename.** `kind_of` reads `<any folder>/gh pr checks ...` as the watch and the launcher starts that file, not the `gh` on PATH; the watch carries no secret and the identity is withheld, so the file runs as any program of the owner's account would. Check: the same exact-name rule for `gh` as BUG-029 gives the agent (the brief packager, BUG-029).

### BUG-029 (its reviews; recorded, not taken)
- **Under BRIDGE_RUN_ALLOW_ANY=1 a cursor-agent given by a path runs as itself.** It is no longer swapped for the real agent, so it gets kind `test`, the full environment (the GitHub identity included), and none of the agent's checks: the key-file refusal, the design ceiling, the review-family check. The variable is test-only and already lets `cmd /c <agent> --key-file` through. Check: base the key-file refusal on `head_of(cmd) == "cursor-agent" or is_agent_argv(cmd)` (the security audit and Review A, BUG-029). **Taken by TASK-032** (stage B5's review, round 1, briefs-launcher C2): the launcher's accepted() refuses a path-qualified cursor-agent under BRIDGE_RUN_ALLOW_ANY.
- **The real agent is found with shutil.which.** `bridge_env.cursor_agent_launcher` tries `cursor-agent.cmd` first, and on Windows its search looks in the launch folder before PATH, so a `cursor-agent.cmd` written into the Workspace or an earlier PATH entry becomes the agent of the next launch, still with the stripped environment and the slot (older than BUG-029). Check: resolve the agent from PATH entries outside the launch folder, or pin it to the `%LOCALAPPDATA%/cursor-agent` install (the security audit, BUG-029).
- **is_agent_argv's directory test is redundant.** No AGENT_NAMES entry holds a separator or a colon, so the whole-token comparison already refuses every path, and a mutant removing the `os.path.dirname` lines survives. Check: keep it as a guard for a future name, or remove it (Review A, BUG-029).
- **The builder's BUG-029 report repeats its first runs' figures as the re-runs'**, as its TASK-029 round-4 report did (Review A, BUG-029; for the stage close's Feedback).

### TASK-031 (its rounds and the supervisor's inspection; recorded, not taken)
- **A killed run leaves the copy, the root `.env` with it, in the owner's home.** `mutation-run-<id>/` under `~/.cursor-bridge/scratch/<project>/` is removed at every end the program reaches and at the next run of that id, not after a kill at the launcher's limit or by `bridge-stop.py`; the `.env` holds local service passwords (KP-032). Check: remove left-behind `mutation-run-*` folders at the start of any run, or copy `.env` only where a mutant's tests read it (the brief packager, TASK-031).
- **The copy has no `.git`.** A test that reads its own repository's history (a baseline commit, `git log`) fails in the copy, so it cannot be a mutant's test; the bridge's own such tests build their repositories under `tmp_path`. Check: name it in TASK-030's text for the mutants file (the brief packager, TASK-031).
- **`<project>` is the Workspace's parent folder's name.** A checkout under `Worktrees/<name>/` shares the scratch folder `Worktrees`; the supervisor runs mutation runs only in its own checkout (rule 39), and each run's `mutation-run-<id>/` is its own. Check: key the folder by the Workspace's path as well (the brief packager, TASK-031).
- **`git archive` applies the repository's export attributes.** An `export-ignore` or `export-subst` in `.gitattributes` changes the checkpoint's copy; the bridge has neither. Check: build the checkpoint's copy from `git ls-tree` and `git cat-file` instead (the supervisor, TASK-031).
- **A test id holding a space is read only to the space.** `parse_report` takes a `-rA` line's id up to its first space, so a parametrized id such as `test_x[a b]` cannot be named in `expect_fail`; no bridge test has one. Check: match the report lines against the expected ids whole (the supervisor, TASK-031). **Retired (stage B5's review, round 1, records-hardening C1):** parse_report is gone since 43daf6e; each test's outcome comes from the program's own plugin (MUTATION_PYTEST_RESULTS), read whole.
- **A refusal found inside the run removes the scratch folder it made.** The link and resolution refusals come after `mutation-run-<id>/` is created; it is removed in the same run, and nothing is written in the Workspace, so only the order differs from the brief's "before any copy" (the supervisor, TASK-031).
- **The builder's round-3 window test opened a visible console to prove there was none.** It started the program with `CREATE_NEW_CONSOLE`, its flag argument spelled `"creation" + "flags"`, which the suite's scan (`tests/test_tests_windowless.py`) reads as no flag at all; the supervisor changed the launch to the suite's windowless default, and the test still kills the mutant without the flag (M06). Round 1's probe test spells the same key that way with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`'s value. For the stage close's Feedback: a scan by string can be passed by spelling, so the rule's test reads launches syntactically (the supervisor, TASK-031).
- **The builder left Requirement 11's cases and nine of ten by-hand mutants to the supervisor in round 1, and reported `Assumed, not verified: none` in round 2.** Round 2's own checkpoint wording had a defect round 3 fixed. For the stage close's Feedback (the supervisor, TASK-031).

### TASK-031 (round 3's reviews; past the owner's three-round ceiling, recorded, not taken)
- **Taken in round 4, the blocking findings:** the kill read from pytest's terminal text (B1), a new test id in an existing file refused at the checkpoint (B2), a listed item judged whole there (B3), keys read from the Workspace after the copy (B4), archive member names unchecked (B5), the scratch containment compared case-sensitively (S1, both reviewers), the cache file itself unchecked for a link (S2, both reviewers), `untracked: ["."]` copying `.git` (S3), an expected survivor's kill counted killed (B6), malformed fields crashing (B7), a pytest configuration above the copy read (B8), and the bound's kill able to hang (handoff/TASK-031-r4.md).
- **The mutants file itself is not checked for a link.** A mutants file under `run/supervisor/` that is a link to a file outside the Workspace is parsed, and its field names and ids are echoed in refusals; the supervisor writes that file. Check: refuse it as the cache file is (the security audit, round 3).
- **A mutant id is not checked against `MUTANT_ID_RX`.** An id holding a newline prints lines shaped like the summary's into the stdout the supervisor reads; the exit code is unaffected. Check: refuse an id outside the pattern (the security audit, round 3).
- **`--checkpoint` is resolved twice.** The ref is verified with `rev-parse` and passed to `git archive` again as written, so a ref moved in between names another commit. Check: resolve it once to a SHA (the security audit, round 3).
- **A file with mixed newlines.** `crlf` is true when the file holds any CRLF, so an `old` in an LF region is converted and not found, and the rewrite changes every other newline (Review A, round 3).
- **A tracked file missing from the working tree.** A mutant of such a file ends in FileNotFoundError, exit 3, rather than a refusal or `not applied` (Review A, round 3).
- **The plugin's namespace-package branch is unreachable for a `.py` file.** `find_spec` of a module file always has an origin; `test_r041_namespace_package_resolution_refused` exercises the origin branch (Review A, round 3).
- **A cache line whose `mutant` or `key` is a list or an object** raises TypeError outside the `try`, exit 3, where the brief says such a line is ignored (Review A, round 3).
- **The parser's description omits that an internal error exits 3 and that `--checkpoint` otherwise exits 0** (Requirement 1: the exit codes of Requirement 9; Review A, round 3).
- **Builder tests weaker than their names.** `test_r041_done_c_mutated_file_reruns` passes whether `calc` reran or was cached (the supervisor's `test_task031_req7_a_change_reruns_only_the_mutants_it_keys` holds it); the default-scratch test compares the temporary home with itself, the real home being patched before it reads; the timeout test skips its pid check when the pid file is absent; the killed-run test does not assert that m2's pytest process ended; the tracked-link test has no junction fallback and skips on this Windows account; several docstrings name R-041 without a clause (Review A, round 3).
- **The supervisor's driver checks the restored bytes once, at the end, and its pytest runs have no timeout** (Review A, round 3). Check: compare after each restore, bound each run.

### TASK-031 (round 4; past the owner's three-round ceiling, recorded, not taken)
- **Taken in round 5:** `run_process` closed a pipe a reader thread was blocked on, so a process outside the killed tree holding the pipes kept the run waiting past the bound (round 4's item 12 not met; a defeated safety mechanism; handoff/TASK-031-r5.md).
- **The checkpoint's collection refusal reads pytest's text.** `checkpoint_collect_refused` finds the module by matching `No module named '<name>'` in pytest's output, the kind of reading round 4 removed from the kill; a message worded otherwise reads as `not run` rather than a refusal. Check: take the collection error from the outcomes plugin's collect report (the supervisor, round 4).
- **A listed file whose checkpoint run timed out or left no outcome record prints nothing.** Only an id prints `not run` there; a file item drops out of the output, and the exit is 0 when nothing else passed. Check: print `<file>: not run` (the supervisor, round 4).
- **The plugin folder comes first on PYTHONPATH and holds the copy.** `mutation-run-<id>/` holds the plugins and the copy as `tree/`, so the copy is importable as a namespace package `tree`, and the plugin files as top-level modules; a project module of those names that is not a regular package is shadowed. Check: keep the plugins in a folder of their own (the supervisor, round 4).
- **`taskkill /T` does not reach a process whose parent has exited.** Such a process outlives the bound until the run ends; under the launcher's `--kind driver` the Job object ends it then, a run started directly leaves it running. Check: a Job object around each pytest run on Windows (the supervisor, round 4).
- **A taskkill that reaches its own 30 s timeout ends the run as an internal error.** `kill_tree`'s `subprocess.run(..., timeout=30)` raises `TimeoutExpired`, which `run_process` does not catch, so the run exits 3 with `mutation-run: internal error` rather than the mutant's `error (timed out)`; the exit code is right, the message is not (the brief packager, round 5).

### TASK-031 (rounds 4 and 5's reviews; past the owner's three-round ceiling, recorded, not taken)
- **Taken in round 6, the blocking findings:** a scratch folder inside the Workspace named through the extended or the administrative-share path form (the security audit, B-1); an `untracked` entry reaching `.git` by another Windows name (B-2); the pytest configuration found by file name rather than as pytest finds it (B-3 and Review A, convergent); a parametrized or class id judged `not run` at the checkpoint (Review A); a collection error beside a kill read as a kill (Review A); a pytest still alive after the kill's wait, its results read as the next mutant's (Review A and the security audit, convergent); the builder's two pipe-holder tests holding pytest's capture file rather than the pipes (Review A; a test weaker than its name) (handoff/TASK-031-r6.md). **The last item's class (stage B5's review, round 1, records-hardening S5):** none of the five; the round, past the by-hand ceiling, took the two weak tests as a gate-integrity should-fix beside its blocking findings, a breach of the ceiling's grading, recorded here; the other weak builder tests of rounds 5 to 7 stayed recorded.
- **The cache file is checked, then opened.** A link swapped in between `validate_cache_file` and the append's `open` is followed. Check: compare `os.fstat` of the opened file with the checked one, `O_NOFOLLOW` on POSIX (the security audit, round 5; Review A).
- **A leftover copy the startup removal could not remove is reused.** Since round 5 a holder can outlive the run with its working folder in the copy; the next run's `rmtree(..., ignore_errors=True)` leaves it and copies into it. Check: exit 3 when `mutation-run-<id>/` still exists after the removal (the security audit, round 5).
- **The abandoned reader threads buffer the holder's output without a limit; on POSIX each timeout leaves two pipe descriptors open.** Both end with the holder (the security audit, round 5).
- **A commit tree entry `.git` is extracted into the checkpoint's copy, and an `untracked` folder holding a nested repository's `.git` is copied whole** (the security audit, round 5).
- **On a case-insensitive macOS volume the scratch containment compares case-sensitively** (the security audit, round 5; macOS is not a target today).
- **A refusal of the cache file leaves an empty `mutation-run-<id>/tree/`.** The check runs after the folder is made and outside the `try/finally` that removes it (Review A, round 5).
- **Round 4's archive checks:** the neither-file-nor-folder branch is untested (a hard-link member is refused before it), and a file member named `.` ends in an internal error rather than a refusal (Review A, round 5).
- **Builder tests weaker than their names, round 4:** `test_r041_done_c_expect_fail_change_reruns` asserts only `run=1`, not each rerun's outcome; the supervisor's item-12 test does not assert that the hold happened, and its docstring names two 30-second waits where round 5 allows three (Review A, round 5).
- **The MUTATION_PYTEST_RESULTS row says "each pytest run"; the resolution check's run does not set it** (Review A, round 5; corrected in the row).

- **The trailing dot, trailing space and colon rule is in `path_bad`, so it holds for every path field, where the brief named `untracked` entries only.** A tracked `file`, `tests` or `new_tests` path of that shape is refused too; git keeps no such name on Windows, so nothing the program could run before is refused now (the supervisor, round 6; named to the reviewers).

### TASK-031 (round 6's reviews; past the owner's three-round ceiling, recorded, not taken)
- **Taken in round 7, the blocking findings:** a mutant id reaching a file path, so an id such as `x\..\..\..\victim` deleted and replaced a `.json` file above the scratch folder (the security audit, N-1; security and data integrity); the pytest configuration read with interpolation, so a `%` in a pytest section ended the run as an internal error where round 5 ran it (Review A and the security audit, convergent, N-2; behaviour against the brief); the builder's grandchild pipe-holder test leaving its holder behind and asserting only the bound (Review A; behaviour against handoff/TASK-031-r6.md item 7) (handoff/TASK-031-r7.md).
- **The NT prefix `\??\` is not refused as a `--scratch`.** `abspath` makes it a folder named `??` on the drive, whose creation fails as an internal error, nothing created. Check: refuse it with the other prefixes (the security audit, round 6).
- **The toml conflict rule is stricter than pytest's.** A `[tool.pytest]` table with an empty `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` is refused; pytest 9.1.1 refuses only a non-empty one (findpaths.py:129) (the security audit and Review A, round 6).
- **A `setup.cfg` holding a plain `[pytest]` section is passed over for the empty file**, where pytest 9 stops with an error, so the mutants run under a configuration the gate cannot run (the security audit and Review A, round 6).
- **A configuration file that is not UTF-8 ends the run as an internal error rather than exit 2** (the security audit, round 6). Taken in round 7 for `.ini` and `.cfg` files with N-2.
- **`-c` always names the root's configuration**; pytest without `-c` searches upward from its arguments' common ancestor, so a `tests/pytest.ini` the gate's own run would read is not read here (Review A, round 6; the brief's B-3 design).
- **The checkpoint loop does not stop on a pytest still alive after an item's timeout** (the security audit, round 6).
- **An untracked hard link to a file inside `.git` is not caught**: the identity check compares with `.git` itself (the security audit, round 6).
- **The default scratch is refused when the home folder is a UNC path (a roaming profile)**; the brief named `--scratch` only (Review A, round 6).
- **The alive-after-kill message names the mutant's bound where the program waited up to 60 s more** (Review A, round 6).
- **Builder tests weaker than their names, round 6:** `test_r041_pytest_survives_kill_stops_run` matches `"3" in stderr` for the bound in seconds; two upper bounds were loosened to `< 125`, the harness timeout, where `< bound + 75` would still catch a regression (Review A, round 6).
- **The driver lacks a mutant for the colon rule, the per-run results path (item 6's first half, untested) and the second identity walk; its exception path restores in `finally` without the byte check** (Review A, round 6).
- **`run_process_still_alive_after_timeout` is a module-global side channel**; a fifth return value would be simpler (Review A, round 6).

### TASK-031 (round 7; the supervisor's inspection and mutation run; recorded, not taken)
- **A `.ini` or `.cfg` configuration is read by `configparser`, pytest's by `iniconfig`; where they differ the program refuses.** Duplicate sections or keys, a key without `=`, continuation and comment placement parse differently; each such file ends the run with exit 2, failing closed. Corrected after round 7's security audit: not every difference fails closed; a section header followed by a comment holding `]` (`[pytest] # note]`) is the pytest section to iniconfig and another section to configparser, so the run took another file and recorded a false kill (taken in round 8, handoff/TASK-031-r8.md). Round 7 makes a `.ini` or `.cfg` that does not decode a refusal too; a `pyproject.toml` or `pytest.toml` that does not decode was not tried (the builder's assumption, round 7; accepted by the supervisor).
- **M53 (the results file named from the mutant id) is not equivalent, as the supervisor first recorded.** `MUTANT_ID_RX` sets no length, so a plain id of 240 characters names a results file past the 255-character limit on one name and its mutant becomes an error (Review A, round 7); the supervisor's test `test_task031_r7_n1_a_long_plain_mutant_id_runs_its_mutant` kills it. M21 and M35 stay equivalent (round 6).
- **A survive reason, and a cached outcome's `time` field, are printed raw**, so a reason holding a newline forges a summary line on stdout; the exit code is unaffected (the security audit, round 7).
- **The run id is checked with `.match`**, so on POSIX a mutants file named with a trailing newline passes; it stays inside the scratch and cache folders (the security audit, round 7).
- **The grandchild pipe-holder fixture writes its pytest's pid after starting the holder**, so a bound reached between the two leaves no pid file and the holder running (Review A, round 7).
- **Round 7's report did not run its verification command 7 as written** (`tasklist` failed under Git Bash; `Get-Process` shows no titles) (Review A, round 7).
- **The grandchild pipe-holder test's `finally` ends a pid it read from a file, which the system may have reused** if both processes ended long before; the test polls them dead first, so the window is the poll's 10 s (the supervisor, round 7).

### TASK-031 (round 8; the builder's assumptions; recorded, not taken)
- **Corrected after round 8's Review A: the supervisor's claim here, that every other header difference makes one parser raise, was false.** A column-0 line `[pytest] x` (or `[pytest] x # c`) that no longer ends in `]` once its comment is cut is, to iniconfig, a continuation of the key above it, and to configparser the section `pytest`; round 8's check passed it and the run recorded a false kill. A second shape: iniconfig splits lines with `str.splitlines`, so a `[pytest]` header after a file separator (`\x1c`) or a form feed is a section to pytest and none to configparser. Both taken in round 9 (handoff/TASK-031-r9.md), which reads these files with iniconfig itself, as pytest does, rather than emulating it. The builder had listed the first shape as not verified (the builder, Review A and the security audit, round 8; convergent).
- **A non-pytest section whose name holds `#`, `;` or `]` refuses the whole file**, which pytest would read; exit 2 with the file named (the builder, round 8).
- **The installed iniconfig is 2.3.0 where requirements.lock pins 2.3.1**, so the local suite and the builder's reading of the source ran on 2.3.0; CI installs from the lock (Review A, round 8). Since round 9 the program reads with the iniconfig the gate's pytest imports, so within one environment the two cannot differ; the local environment still differs from the lock (the supervisor, round 9). Not quite: a project's own top-level `iniconfig` comes first for the gate's pytest; taken in round 10 (handoff/TASK-031-r10.md).

### TASK-031 (round 9's reviews; past the owner's three-round ceiling, recorded, not taken)
- **Taken in round 10, convergent:** a project's top-level `iniconfig.py` or `iniconfig/` is imported by the gate's `python -m pytest`, started at the project's top, before the installed one, while the program reads with the installed iniconfig, so the two could pick different files (Review A and the security audit, round 9).
- **An unreadable `tox.ini` or `setup.cfg` now ends the run as an internal error**, exit 3, where configparser passed over it; pytest fails on the same file too (Review A, round 9).
- **pytest also reads every later candidate and stops on one that does not parse**; the program stops at the first file that counts, so with a `.pytest.ini` and an unparsable `tox.ini` it runs mutants where the gate's pytest exits with a usage error. Not a false kill (the security audit, round 9).
- **A `pyproject.toml` or `pytest.toml` is read in binary by `tomllib`, pytest's with universal newlines**, so a lone `\r` is refused where pytest reads the file (fails closed), and a toml file that does not decode ends as an internal error rather than exit 2 (the security audit, round 9).
- **The `.cfg` branch's not-importable refusal had no test**; the supervisor's `test_task031_r9_iniconfig_not_importable_while_reading_setup_cfg_is_refused` covers it (Review A, round 9).

### TASK-031 (round 12's reviews; the by-class line drawn, recorded, not taken)
- **The line drawn (stage B5's review, round 1, records-hardening S4):** past the ceiling, the class is behaviour against R-041, and only a regression or a false kill on an ordinary project layout blocks (handoff/TASK-031-r12.md, this round's decision); a finding that needs a contrived layout, or that fails closed, is recorded below (INVENTORY, Decisions: the by-class line and the shadow refusal).
- **The fixed list leaves out other modules the mutant's pytest imports from the project's top first**: `packaging` (the `minversion` check), `exceptiongroup` (Python 3.10 and older), `colorama` (Windows), and standard modules pytest's configuration step uses such as `shlex` (it splits `addopts`); a project shipping a top-level module of one of those names could make the mutant's pytest read its configuration differently. ### TASK-031 (round 13; CI's stale bytecode, the builder's assumptions; recorded, not taken)
- **Taken in round 13, a failing test and a false result on an ordinary layout:** CI's Windows job on round 12's head reported six mutants `survived` that the tests kill. The cause, confirmed by a probe: a `.pyc` records its source's size and modification time to the second, so a mutant of the same size written within the second of the previous run's compile ran the previous bytecode. Round 13 sets `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` for every pytest the program starts and removes `__pycache__` folders and `.pyc` files from the copy after it is built, narrowed to `__pycache__` folders by round 14 (below) (test_task031_r13_no_test_run_of_the_program_writes_bytecode; mutant M69 killed).
- **The removal of bytecode the copy brought with it is caught by no test** (mutants M70 and M71, the two calls dropped, survive): only a project that commits its `__pycache__`, or lists it as untracked, brings bytecode into the copy, and only a stale one whose recorded size and time match the mutated source would run. A contrived layout under the drawn line (the builder's report, round 13).
- **A linked `__pycache__` (or, in round 13, `.pyc`) in the copy would be left in place** (the removal does not follow links), but the copy holds no link: copy_tracked copies contents (`shutil.copy2`, `copytree` with `symlinks=False`) and the checkpoint's archive admits only regular files and folders, so the link test is a guard that does not fire today (the supervisor, round 13).
- **A committed `__pycache__` removed from the copy no longer contributes to the mutant's key**: a key taken before round 13 over a folder holding committed bytecode differs from one taken after, so such a mutant runs once more; bytecode in `__pycache__` that the copy no longer holds cannot affect the run (the builder's report, round 13; corrected by Review A, round 13: while round 13 also removed loose `.pyc` files, a change to a `.pyc` test fixture no longer changed the key, which round 14's narrowing to `__pycache__` ends).

### TASK-031 (round 13's reviews; the by-class line drawn, recorded, not taken)
- **Taken in round 14, a false kill on an ordinary layout, a defect of the supervisor's round-13 brief:** removing every `.pyc` from the copy deleted the `.pyc` files a project that reads bytecode commits as test data, so its tests failed for a reason that is not the mutant and an equivalent mutant was recorded killed, exit 0 (Review A reproduced it on round 13's program; round 12's reported it survived). A loose `.pyc` beside its source is never read for the module (the importer prefers the `.py`; a sourceless `.pyc` is imported, but a mutant targets a source file, so it is never stale against one), so round 14 removes `__pycache__` folders only (test_task031_r14_a_tracked_pyc_the_tests_read_stays_in_the_copy).
- **`os.walk(followlinks=False)` still descends into a Windows junction** (its entry is not a symbolic link to `DirEntry.is_symlink`), and the link test covers only folders named `__pycache__`, so a junction of another name in the copy would lead the removal into the folder it points to; the security audit's probe deleted bytecode there. The copy holds no link or junction (the bullet above on links), so only another process planting one between the copy and the walk could; a contrived setup. The fix, when taken: prune every linked folder from the walk whatever its name (the security audit, round 13).
- **A `PYTHONPYCACHEPREFIX` in the owner's environment survives into the children**, which then read cached bytecode from outside the copy, keyed by the copy's absolute path; only an entry from before round 13 with the same size and second matches (the security audit and Review A, round 13). The fix, when taken: drop the variable from `pytest_env`.
- **The round-13 test exercises the mutant run's child only**, not the resolution check (a collection) or the checkpoint runs; a later call site that skipped `pytest_env` would go unseen (Review A, round 13).
- **A `__pycache__` folder holding real test data would be removed from the copy** (Review A, round 13). Contrived.
- **The plugin folder's `__pycache__` under the run's work folder lies outside the walk** and relies on the work folder's removal at each run's start; with writing off, only a leftover from before round 13 with the same size and second could matter (Review A, round 13).

### TASK-031 (the CI runner fix, 57ba8a0; recorded, not taken)
- **Taken, a failing test on Linux:** every Ubuntu job of PR #91 lost its runner after about 48 minutes: test_r041_killed_run_keeps_partial_cache started the program with a plain `Popen`, and its cleanup's `os.killpg(os.getpgid(proc.pid))` signalled the test run's own process group, pytest's and the runner's (reproduced on Linux by Review A). The test starts the program in a session of its own on POSIX, and kill_proc_tree never signals the test run's own group (the supervisor; Review A APPROVE). **The POSIX dispositions (stage B5's review, round 1, briefs-task031 C):** the earlier rounds' "CI's Ubuntu job runs it" held only from 57ba8a0, CI run 40532162872 (2578 passed, 51 skipped, read with `-q`, not `-rs`); before it every Ubuntu job lost its runner.
- **kill_proc_tree's new guard falls back to `proc.kill()` on the direct child alone**: a later caller passing a child without a session of its own would leave its grandchildren running, with no warning (Review A).
- **If an assertion of test_r041_killed_run_keeps_partial_cache fails before its marker is written, the mutant pytest it left in its own session polls for the marker for ever**; it holds none of the job's pipes and a CI runner ends it at the job's end, but on a developer's computer it lingers; writing the marker in a `finally` would bound it (Review A).

### TASK-030 (the gate's round 5; recorded with TASK-032's records)
