Both fixed in bridge-run.py and review-guard.py, tests/test_task032_supervisor_r4.py.
- **Taken, round 3's suite (a failing test):** three new test files passed `creationflags=NO_WINDOW` (a variable), which tests/test_tests_windowless.py's scan flags; the flag was dropped so each launch takes conftest.py's windowless default (the supervisor's commit b1ad478).
- **The module docstrings of bridge-run.py and permission-guard.py describe none of the new rules, refusals or exit codes, and review-guard.py's gained one sentence**, though rounds 2 and 3 asked for them and the builder's round-3 report says they were updated (Review A, rounds 2 and 3; D1, B13). The command reference renders the usage of hand-parsed programs from the docstring.
- **The builder's reports claimed work not done** (round 3: C1 and D1; `Assumed, not verified: none` in rounds 3 and 4); KP-010's lesson held: every claim was checked against the diff and the supervisor's tests.
- **Malformed records still crash with a traceback in three shapes, failing closed** (exit 1, nothing started): a `lift_line` list in runs.jsonl (bridge-run.py, the lift lookup's class_used loop); a list `branch` in another ID's rounds file, launcher and guard (bridge_env.py `_valid_rounds_file` never checks `branch`); a `lift_line` list in the guard's own record (review-guard.py, the class-line read of its rounds). The guard's exit 1 reads as GATE-INTEGRITY (Review A, round 3, B1/N2).
- **`launch_info`'s `int()` on a 5,000-digit `.err` exit** would raise a traceback during verify (pre-existing; Review A, round 3, N9).
- **The ID pattern is still written three times** (`_REVIEW_NAME_RX`, `REVIEW_LIFT_RE`, `MERGE_UNIT_RE`), though round 3's C1 asked for one constant and the report says it was done (Review A, round 3, N3).
- **The squash reader under the grammar's pattern reads an ID after a non-ID leading word** (`Fix TASK-001 typo`, `Revert TASK-001: ...`, `Plan: TASK-033 ...`), which lapses that branch's claim early; R6 asked for leading tokens only; no real subject on main is affected today (Review A, round 3, N4).
- **`--healthy` rewrites runs.jsonl from `read_log`, which now drops type-invalid rows**; 0 of the real log's 3,392 rows are affected (Review A, round 3, N5).
- **tests/test_review_guard.py's second ID still uses the branch `task-002b`** (R23, E3; Review A, round 3, N6), and tests/test_bridge_run_design_ceiling.py:47 dropped the `Design rounds above five:` half of the old assertion (S8).
- **The guard's lapse git calls run with a 120 s timeout, not R5's 30 s** (Review A, round 3, N7); `branch_of` decodes without `errors="replace"` (S4).
- **Guard refusals are not backslash-escaped, and the guard's ceiling message names the lifts without their forms** (Review A, round 3, S1); **a refused snapshot still creates `.git/bridge/no-hooks/`** (S2).
- **The prompt carries uncounted work**: an identical-bytes relaunch is free and the prompt is neither logged nor hashed, so instructions can ride in it (Review A, round 3, N8; the security audit); a `prompt_sha256` treated like the brief's bytes would count it.
- **The branch rule trusts records nothing protects**: runs.jsonl has no hash copy and the branch rule reads other IDs' rounds files without the tamper check, so one forged line widens a branch's declared set (the security audit; contrived, a deliberate forgery).
- **The lapse reads the local `main` ref** (a local commit or a tag named `main` forges it), and the closing-parenthesis form under the grammar lapses on a plan-revision subject ending `(TASK-032, ...)`; `refs/remotes/origin/main` and the squash forms only would close it (the security audit; contrived).
- **A wrapper shell drops the backslash separator**: `cmd /c cursor-agent -p "Read handoff\TASK-001.md"` and the powershell form pass the permission guard, and `run/./review/` slips through; deliberate bypasses (`python -c`, `Start-Process`, `npx`) remain in any case (the security audit; backstopped by the guard's count).
- **The launcher reads and writes runs.jsonl in its working directory**, so a launch from another folder with `--workspace <checkout>` sees an empty log (the security audit; realistic, backstopped by the guard's count); `bridge_env.launcher_log_folder` for both would close it.
- **A `--kind review` launch is refused only for a grammar-valid brief name**: `handoff/fix.md` or a brief placed in a `RESOLUTION-*.md` or `*-rN-report.md` file runs uncounted (the security audit; contrived).
- **A new ID on a new branch has a fresh budget in both programs, and the launcher counts an ID's rounds per branch while the guard counts them per ID** (the security audit; Review A, round 2): the plan's design (clause (b)), which a later plan revision may tighten.
- **The owner's line is read from the working tree with no provenance check** (the security audit; contrived); `git show origin/main:docs/RUN_PARAMETERS.md` would close it.
- **Under `BRIDGE_RUN_ALLOW_ANY=1`, `-- cmd /c cursor-agent ...` runs as a helper with the real home** (pre-existing; the security audit; contrived).
- **`git_common_dir` returning None skips the guard's records in the branch rule** (the security audit; contrived).
- **A branch deleted unmerged and made again under the same name**: the deletion half of the lapse cannot be observed for the branch being used (the supervisor's ruling, round 3's tests).
- **A void review on four of `verify`'s GATE-INTEGRITY exits still spends its round** (review-guard.py, `cmd_verify`: the snapshot rewritten, the configuration or a hook changed, the branch held in another worktree, a checkout that cannot be put back): only its last GATE-INTEGRITY exit calls `record_flagged_launch`, so after a snapshot rewritten, a changed configuration or hook, the branch held in another worktree or a checkout that cannot be put back, the `.err` file's exit 0 counts as the round's reply at the next snapshot, where R11 says a flagged review spends no round (Review A, round 4, B1, and the security audit of round 4, finding 3: two reviewers on one line; the round-4 brief's own wording, "a launch that a `verify` ending GATE-INTEGRITY read", was narrower than R11). Recorded past the ceiling, not taken: each path follows a reviewer's tampering or a worktree left behind, two of them end in "verify again", whose second run records the reply or the flag as usual, and the error fails closed (one round of the budget, which the owner's line restores); the fix is `record_flagged_launch` on every GATE-INTEGRITY return, against the round's recorded `time` where the snapshot is untrusted, with a test of the hooks path.
- **Round 1's two spellings**: a same-tree re-snapshot as `TASK-001-r1` renames round 1's entry, and the `.err`-derived reply is then read from `REVIEW-TASK-001-r1.err`, so the round can be re-read on a changed tree; deleting the `.err` file, or a relaunch that exits non-zero over it, reopens an unverified round likewise (Review A, round 4, S1, and the security audit of round 4, findings 1 and 2; a deliberate setup; keeping the round's first recorded name, as `time` is kept, would close the first; the `.err` file is ignored and has no hash copy, so a reply read from it is as strong as that file).
- **`snapshot` drops `flagged_launch` when it replaces a round's entry** (review-guard.py, `cmd_snapshot`'s replaced round entry carries `reply`, `lift`, `lift_line` and `time` forward, not the flag), so after a GATE-INTEGRITY verify, a same-tree re-snapshot, a records commit and a re-snapshot before the next launch are refused as reviewed, though the one review was void (the security audit of round 4, finding 4; fails closed; the fix is to copy the field forward with the others).
- **A non-string `time` or `flagged_launch` in a rounds record raises a traceback** (review-guard.py, `_launch_time_ge_round` and `err_counts_as_reply`, which strip the strings they read), reachable only by rewriting the record and its hash copy, failing closed before any write; `record_flagged_launch` compares the launch with the latest snapshot's `taken` while the reply check uses the round's first `time`, so a launch between the two counts as a reply and cannot be flagged (Review A, round 4, N1, N2).
- **The revised-in-place rule refuses a return to earlier bytes after later bytes completed**, stricter than the round-4 brief's sentence ("no run with the current bytes"); `prior_brief_run_same_sha` duplicates `prior_brief_run` with a hash filter; a same-bytes relaunch of an earlier round's name runs at that name's round, past the ceiling too, by the "adds no round" design (Review A, round 4, N3 to N5).
- **Letter seeding counts past a gap** (review-guard.py, `_letter_snapshot_rounds`): every same-branch letter snapshot is counted, so `review-TASK-001.json`, `-a` and `-c` with no `-b` seed three rounds where handoff/TASK-032-r3.md G9 says the count stops at the first gap (Review B, the gate's round 1, REJECT on this finding alone). Recorded past the ceiling, not taken: the seeding runs once, for an increment in flight when the release installs; a gap needs a skipped letter or a deleted snapshot; the error fails closed (a round more counted, which the owner's line restores); the fix compares each suffix with the next expected letter (`''`, `a`, `b`, ...) and stops at the first that differs, since the suffixes are sorted as strings.
- **Two records of one branch in the same second tie** (bridge_env.py `_branch_events`, its sort by a time kept to the second): the branch's first record, which sets its declared IDs, is then the one the bridge folder lists first, alphabetical on Windows and not on Linux. PR #94's Linux job failed two supervisor tests on it (they took two snapshots within a second; fixed in the tests, f897795); in the loop a snapshot follows a Review B run minutes after the last. Seeded rounds carry an empty time, kept by a later snapshot, so seeded rounds of two IDs on one branch always tie and sort before every launcher row. A launcher row already sorts before a guard record of the same second (the stable sort, launcher rows appended first); a tie-break on the round's name after the time would make the guard's order the same everywhere. A tie usually fails closed; it fails open only for an owner-lifted second ID whose brief declares a larger merge unit, snapshotted in the same second (Review A, the gate's round 2).
- **The test suite writes into the real launcher log**: every suite run adds `kind: test` rows (limits 3 and 30) to the checkout's run/launcher/runs.jsonl: 2,948 of its 3,010 test rows, from 2026-10-12 to 2026-10-28, 1,473 of them at exit 0 and so marked healthy, which is what the launcher learns the test kind's ceiling from (bridge-run.py's learned limit); the run log is therefore mostly the tests' for that kind (pre-existing; seen at TASK-032's verify, counted by Review A; the test that launches with the checkout as its folder is not in TASK-032's five test files and is not yet named).
- **The process (the supervisor):** the builder delivered rounds 1 and 2 incomplete (17 and 35 of about 60 test cases); round 3 ran on 231 acceptance tests the supervisor wrote, and passed them. An increment whose Done when lists some 60 test cases is more than one builder run delivers; the plan could name the supervisor's tests from the start.

### TASK-033 (the gate's rounds 1 to 4; past the owner's three-round ceiling from round 4, recorded, not taken)
- **Taken, round 4 (past the ceiling, `Blocking class: safety mechanism`, with two of the class behaviour):** the drain of an over-cap transcript line called `past_deadline()` and did nothing with the answer (`if past_deadline(): pass`), so the session read's bound did not hold on a long line (Review A, round 3, B1, and the security audit of round 3, B: converged); the letter rule asked the disk (`os.path.isfile`) where round 3's Requirement 9 asked the tracked list, so an uncommitted `handoff/TASK-040b.md` took `REVIEW-TASK-040b.err` away from TASK-040 (Review A B2, the audit's D); `R-nnn` was tested before the ID pattern, so under the grammar `docs: x (R-027)` merged nothing (Review A B3, the audit's C). Fixed in pace-report.py and bridge_env.py, tests/test_task033_supervisor_r4.py.
- **The review guard's own git runner reads text** (review-guard.py:100-111, `text=True`): a lone carriage return in a squash subject reaches `_claim_lapsed` as a line break, so the subject is split where the shared reader would not split it (the security audit, round 3, A). Contrived: a squash subject on protected `main` is a GitHub pull-request title, which is one line; review-guard.py was outside the round's Scope. Reading bytes and decoding with `errors="replace"`, as pace-report.py does, would close it.
- **`git ls-files` and the HARDENING numstat are read as text and split with `splitlines`, `core.quotePath` unpinned** (pace-report.py; Review A, round 3, S1, and the security audit's advisory): a tracked brief whose path holds a non-ASCII or control character is quoted by git and never matches its ID. Every handoff path is ASCII today; `-z` and a NUL split would close it.
- **The HARDENING numstat reads `main`, not `refs/heads/main`** (the security audit, round 3): a tag or a tracked path named `main` makes it ambiguous, which fails that column only, as a git failure; the commit read already uses the full ref with `--` after it.
- **bridge_env.py `squash_merged_ids`'s `git log main` (reached only through `_claim_lapsed`; plan-check.py and pace-report.py run their own `git log` and share only the parser, `squash_merged_ids_from_lines`) has no `--` after the ref and inherits GIT_DIR and its kin** (the security audit, round 3; pre-existing, TASK-032's): an ambiguous `main` or a GIT_DIR set by a parent makes the lapse's read fail, which keeps the claim (fails safe).
- **The shared parser differs from round 1's on a lone `(#N)` only**: the security audit fuzzed 1.2 million subjects and found no difference in the merged set, the difference being the parenthesis read as a pull-request number rather than an empty item list (the audit, round 3, advisory).
- **The builder's reports claimed work not done** (round 3: "drains ... with deadline checks", which the code did not make, and mutations 4 and 7 reported caught that survived when Review A ran them; Review A, round 3, S2): ISS-017 recurred; every claim was checked against the diff and the supervisor's tests, and round 4's brief asked the report to state only what the diff shows.
- **Round 3's supervisor tests could not tell two of their pins apart** (Review A, round 3, S3): the cap test passed with `readline()` given no size, and a folder named `main` could not tell `refs/heads/main --` from `refs/heads/main`; round 4's tests pin both (every transcript readline given a size of at most LINE_CAP + 1, a tracked file at `refs/heads/main`), and the letter and `R-nnn` readings, which no test held.
- **Unused and duplicated names** (Review A, round 3, considers): `_MAX_JSONL_LINE` is unused; `_COMMIT_LOG_CMD` repeats the format string the commit read builds; `_trail_subject_search` is a bare alias of the shared reader; `_iso_week` is called outside the `try` that guards the other date arithmetic; plan-check.py's `isinstance` branch on the subject is always taken.
- **`GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH` needs git 2.44 or later** (Review A, round 3, consider): an older git ignores it, and a partial clone could then fetch during a read; the bridge's computers and CI run newer gits.
- **Round 4's notes** (Review A, round 4, APPROVE; the security audit of round 4 PASS, nothing new): `_read_line_capped`'s check before the first read repeats `handle_file`'s check at the loop's top, and bridge_env.py's subject parser repeats the full match `_parenthesis_bare_item` already makes, both redundant and harmless; where `handoff/` is untracked the tracked list is empty, so a stage review's lettered `.err` is not counted toward that stage's reading over the ceiling (the tracked-list rule; that project's rounds column prints `n/a`; a test of a `STAGE-<name>` letter `.err` in such a project would settle it); tests/test_task033_supervisor_r4.py's last docstring cites "Requirement 4" of a brief with three, the `--` pin being a Context bullet; handoff/TASK-033-r4-report.md holds non-ASCII punctuation (the ASCII rule covers the two programs).

The file was saved at 07:41:51 UTC, during the review (launched 07:31:55); the administrator session's transcript shows its edits recording, at the owner's word, the weekly scan's wait for release T and Dependabot switched on for the account, and those words are in the file: not the reviewer. - **Inputs:** jscpd 5.4.1 over bridge, tests and tools (104 clones); those touching a file stage B5 changed (e671e39 to 8a29708) ranked by refactor-scout against the 400-line budget.

### BUG-030 (stage B5's review, round 1's eight program causes; the fix's gate, rounds 1 to 3, recorded, not taken)
- **Round 1 (REJECT by Review A, FAIL by the security audit), fixed in round 2:** only ImportError was caught around mutation-run.py's two imports, so a bridge_env.py that cannot be compiled still ended with a traceback and exit 1, and the imports moved into `run_main` left the module loaded in-process with `bridge_env = None` (Review A, Blocking 1 and Should fix 3); scope-check.py's builder window took a bool or a negative `waited` (Review A, Blocking 2; the audit's advisory); the checkpoint copy of the working tree's conftest.py files followed a link, an untracked conftest.py being checked by nothing else (the audit, Blocking).
- **Round 2 (APPROVE, PASS), its one finding fixed in round 3:** the internal-error line named no module where the exception's text does not, and kept only the last of two failures (Review A, Should fix 1, ruled blocking as behaviour against the brief; the audit's advisory on the same line).
- **The check and the copy are two steps** (the audit, round 2; Review A, round 2, consider): a link swapped in after `link_on_path` and before `shutil.copy2` is followed, in the conftest copy as in `archive_checkpoint`'s untracked copy before it; comparing `os.fstat` of the opened source with the `os.lstat` taken at the check (or `O_NOFOLLOW` on POSIX) would close it. It needs a writer racing inside the Workspace during the run.
- **A hard link is not a link to the check** (the audit, round 2): `bridge_env.is_link` does not see a hard-linked conftest.py, untracked path or tracked file; refusing `st_nlink > 1` would close it, possible on one volume (a Linux runner), not across this computer's two drives.
- **The dangling-link refusal is untested** (the audit, round 2): no test makes a link that leads nowhere at a conftest path, so `os.path.lexists` going back to `os.path.isfile` would pass both tests; such a link copies nothing either way.
- **A Workspace folder that is itself a link refuses every checkpoint run with a working-tree conftest.py** (Review A, round 2, consider): `link_on_path` checks its `stop` folder too, as `validate_untracked` already did for untracked paths; it fails closed with a named line.
- **`seconds` in the builder window takes a bool or a negative number** (round 1's brief, out of scope; the audit, round 2): a negative `seconds` shortens the window as a negative `waited` did; anyone who can write the run log can also delete the row.
- **Carried from round 1's audit:** `HANDOFF_REPORT_NAME` matches any name ending `-report.md`; after the slot wait the launcher compares the brief's bytes again, not its other judgements (the branch, the round, the class lines), which a run logged meanwhile could change.
- **The supervisor launched round 3's builder while the round-2 suite ran** (2026-10-28): the installed launcher (2026.10.25a) has no heavy slot, which release B adds (TASK-029), so the builder started at once and edited mutation-run.py under the running suite; that run was stopped and the suite run again on round 3's code. Until release B installs, a builder starts only after a suite ends.
- **Round 3 (APPROVE, PASS), recorded:** an exception whose text holds a newline prints the internal-error line over several lines (the brief takes the text as Python gives it); the supervisor's test checks the module names and the text by substring, not the exact `cannot import <name>: <type>: <text>` form, its `; ` separator or its order; the `tomllib` block stands after `from datetime import ...`, not where `import tomllib` stood, still before the `sys.path` insert. A non-ASCII exception text cannot crash the print: `__main__` sets stderr to `errors="backslashreplace"` (the audit, round 3).

### BUG-032 (stage B5's review, round 2's four program causes; the fix's gate, rounds 1 to 4, the fourth above the ceiling under its Blocking class line; recorded, not taken)
- **Round 1 (REJECT by Review A, FAIL by the security audit), corrected in round 2:** with two copied conftest.py files failing to load, the second resolution check's answer was thrown away, so the tests behind the second were not named and the tests behind neither lost the check's refusals (both, Blocking); a conftest unchanged since the checkpoint was excused (Review A, Should fix; the audit's advisory; convergent); the excusing line was the first match anywhere in stdout and stderr (the audit); a `refactor/` branch admitted every REFAC name, so the pass's snapshot could be taken again under a fresh name with fresh guard rounds (the audit's advisory; Review A's design note; convergent).
- **The supervisor's own test was wrong once (round 2's tests, 2026-10-29):** the first spoof test called `pytest.exit` in a test module, which pytest reports as a collection error (exit 2), not a conftest load failure (exit 4); it failed on the code before BUG-032 too. Rewritten, after checking by hand, as a conftest that writes the line to stderr before it fails, which pytest passes through ahead of its own line.
- **Round 2 (REJECT, FAIL; convergent), corrected in round 3:** a failing conftest still chose the last exact line: it ends its forged line where pytest's begins (Review A) or writes it at exit (the audit), naming another conftest whose tests were then hidden; a git failure excused a conftest (both, Should fix and advisory); numbered REFAC snapshot names never launched opened fresh rounds (the audit, Blocking).
- **Round 3 (the ceiling's last; REJECT, FAIL; convergent, of the five: behaviour against the brief and a defeated safety mechanism), corrected in round 4 under its Blocking class line:** the single-file runs' conftests went into one shared set and items were blocked by that set, so a clear file under a shared parent conftest was listed `not run` and a new test passing on the old code gave exit 0. The supervisor named the point to both reviewers before they read; round 3's brief miscounted its tests (fifteen for fourteen).
- **Round 4 (APPROVE, PASS), recorded:** the file part of an item is computed twice in `main`'s checkpoint branch (Review A, Consider: one helper would keep the keys and the lookups together); no round-4 test reports a conftest off the file's way, the refusal that makes "blocked by its own run" mean "blocked by a conftest behind itself" (Review A, Consider; settled by reading, and by rounds 2 and 3's tests indirectly).
- **A lead, older than BUG-032 (the audit, round 4; reproduced on origin/main):** every run of a checkpoint check uses the one scratch copy, so a new test module imported at collection, or a conftest that fails in a single-file run, can rewrite another listed file or a changed conftest in the copy: a test passing on the old code is then reported failing or `not run`, exit 0. It gives a builder nothing its own test file cannot do; re-extracting the copy per run, or checking the hashes of the listed files and copied conftests before each, would close it.
- **Recorded, not taken (outside the five, rounds 3 and 4):** the reason a blocked file prints can be forged by code in that file's own run (a test module can name any changed copied conftest on its way; only that file is affected); a `refactor/<stage>` snapshot is still admitted under the branch's declared ID, the first candidate's `REFAC-<nnn>`, a second ceiling for the branch (a multi-ID task branch has the same property); `git ls-tree` resolves the path from the Workspace while `rev-parse <commit>:<path>` resolves from the repository's top, equal because the run refuses a Workspace that is not the top, failing safe otherwise; a whole-file item whose own run gives no results file prints nothing (TASK-031's code; a `::` item prints `not run`).
- **The colour setting (round 3, accepted):** the resolution check's run sets `PY_COLORS=0` and drops `FORCE_COLOR` so pytest's own line is plain; a conftest that turns colour back on only gets the run refused.

- **A lead (briefs-launcher S1), recorded with its rule:** a launcher started inside a `cursor-agent` run reads the builder's stripped environment, where HOME and USERPROFILE are the agent's home folder, so it takes the agent home's `.cursor-bridge/heavy.lock`, not the computer's slot, against TASK-029's "one slot on the computer". The rule until a fix, held by the supervisor and stated in no text a builder reads: a builder never starts the launcher (a brief's Done list runs pytest directly, never through the launcher, and the supervisor runs every suite); the fix is the slot's path read from a variable the launcher sets for its children, or the real profile folder.
- **Recorded, not taken (programs, Consider):** conformance-check.py imports `tomllib` unguarded (Python before 3.11 ends with a traceback, exit 1); its `_load_bridge_env` writes `__pycache__` where its docstring says it never writes; pace-report.py's `_report_rounds` reads no `<ID>-report.md`, so BUG-029's rounds drop out of the mean. The instruction is the same either way, so the label is no finding of the five classes and did not block (the by-class line past the ceiling, INVENTORY, Decisions): the pull request merged on round 5's verify, and the wording is corrected in the calibration's records (PR #109).
- **The lesson, for the next stamp or any pull request that changes the status file or the version:** the two failures escaped in two ways. test_r001_status_names_its_own_merge runs only when the GitHub variables say the run is a pull request into `main`, so a plain local run skips it. test_r027_r033_bridge_own_status_file has no such gate (it runs the repository's conformance check against the repository's VERSION) and would have failed on any local run, but test_calibrate_program.py was in none of TASK-027's local selections. So on the final head, before the first push, run every test that reads docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md, project.json or VERSION, or the full suite, with `GITHUB_REF=refs/pull/<n>/merge`, `GITHUB_BASE_REF=main` and `GITHUB_EVENT_NAME=pull_request` set. A candidate for the Issues file at stage B6's close.

## Release T (the change cycle's design revision, 2026-11-01)

- **`APPDATA` and `LOCALAPPDATA` are the owner's in every agent run** (D-004's first deviation, closed in text by TASK-036: KP-038 says so; the redirect stays open). `stripped_env` redirects the four home variables and not these two, so a tool that reads its store through them finds the owner's (gh is hidden by `GH_CONFIG_DIR`). Check: a probe in a Claude Code kept apart (KP-008) that points both at the agent home's `AppData` skeleton and runs the shim, `cursor-agent --list-models` and a builder round, since the shim and cursor-agent live under `LOCALAPPDATA`; then the redirect as a program item of the maintainer's next program release; KP-038 narrowed meanwhile (R-054).
- **The guard's records and the launcher's log are agent-writable and outside `verify`'s protected prefixes** (D-004's open points): `<git common dir>/bridge/` (rounds, verified marks, `premerge/<sha>`, which the permission guard trusts) sits in the checkout's `.git`, which `verify` checks for `config` and the hooks only, and `run/launcher/runs.jsonl` (the ceilings, the last builder model) is listed as LEFT BEHIND, not GATE-INTEGRITY. Deliberate-act findings (KP-032: not a sandbox). Check: `verify` hashing the records folder and the log at the snapshot and flagging a change, a program item; until then the sha256 copies outside the checkout cover the snapshot alone.
- **The launcher has no key file for a driver** (D-004's objection, decided for `--strip`): a mutation run of a suite that needs a variable beyond the project's `.env` cannot run stripped, so it runs unstripped by a recorded Decision. Check: the launcher accepting `--key-file` for the driver kind as it does for the test kind, with the same refusals, a program item for the maintainer's next program release; no project of the four needs it today (their suites read `.env`).
- **check:components cannot see a load by file.** diagram-check.py's py_imports reads import and from lines only; a hyphenated program can be loaded only through importlib.util.spec_from_file_location, so the five sibling loads D-002 draws (bridge-run.py of roster-check.py; conformance-check.py and inventory-check.py of workflow-check.py; session-start.py of workflow-check.py; pace-report.py of evaluate-reports.py) and tools/dump-cli.py's loads pass the check silently, and a new one would too. Covered by D-002's named review item (diff-reviewer reads such a load as an edge the diff must draw or raise). Check: the check learns the file-load form with a sibling path, a program item for the maintainer's next program release (named in release T's CHANGELOG entry); until then the review item is the enforcement (diagram-specialist's open point, D-002's draft).
- **The design text read the dependency rule narrower than the code.** The first D-002 draft reported five deviations against "programs share nothing but bridge_env"; the loads had each been built and accepted through the gate (the shared judge: INVENTORY, Decisions, the session-start hook's threat model; stage B5's lead on the shared judge). Conventions §8's second case: the design corrected, the diagram drawn with the edges, no deviation recorded. Lesson: an as-built retrofit states the dependency rule from the gate's record before the specialist draws, not from the plan's prose.

### TASK-041, the security floor's conventions, the weekly scan's text and the stripped launches (2026-11-02)
- **The Scope widened by two pin files** (Review A, round 1): tests/test_release_t_pitfalls.py counted two D-004 rows in docs/diagrams/DEVIATIONS.md and tests/test_loop_text_pace.py pinned three sentences naming the Pace section alone; both pin text this increment moved and are re-taken in it (db0f166), the plan's Scope line having missed them.
- **The weekly window, redrawn in the increment** (Review A, round 1 Blocking and the re-read's two Should fix): the design's `--created ">=<date>"` window always held the run the previous evaluation read, so a late or missing schedule read green; the window is now the runs created after the previous section's `last run read:` time, and a standing finding is carried as `finding standing: run <id>` until a later run ends `success` (db0f166, a3caabd; docs/DESIGN.md's weekly section says the same, the owner told in the report, rule 58).
- **The observation proves less than a rule match** (Review A, round 1, Consider, taken in the text): the absence of a prompt does not tell the allow rule from auto mode's classifier, so the host row says the observation does not contradict the assumption. Check: a prompt or a refusal at a later stripped launch; then the two heavy-launch rules gain the `--strip` forms and tests/permission_rules.py's model is narrowed, as the design's assumption paragraph says.
- **Review B's round 1 (REJECT, fixed for round 2):** tests/test_release_t_strip.py failed in a shell an agent opens inside a stripped run, Git for Windows' login shell putting its own `SSH_ASKPASS` helper back; that one value is excused, every other name and value still failing the test, and a second test holds `stripped_env` to withholding the name (run/supervisor/task041_strip_sim.py proved both ways). The scanner-failure kind's "no finding stands" (Review A's confirming read, Consider, and Review B's Should fix, convergent) and "a later run of the window" against the carry sentence's "later window" are put right: a finding stands until a later run ends `success`, in that window or a later one, and a scanner failure is reported beside it.
- **Review B's round 2 (REJECT, fixed for round 3):** where the previous section gave no `last run read:` time the window took every listed run, so on a project's first reading the previous Sunday's success, which the `>=` date still lists, read a missing or queued schedule as green; the window then takes only the runs created on a later day than that date (the Blocking finding), and a reading that completes no run with no earlier value writes `last run read: none`, every listed run being the next window, so a run queued at that reading or a rerun made after it is still read (Review A's read of the fix, Should fix; its second, the design's missing carry clause, taken too). Its Should fix: the text said a run still in progress is read the next Sunday, while one older than the newest completed run is passed by that run's time and never read; the procedure now states the drop, a concurrent older run being no ordinary week (the Decision is the supervisor's, by-class).
- **Recorded, Review A's read of the fix, Consider:** a Sunday evaluation missed altogether leaves the unread previous Sunday's run in the window, so a missing schedule reads green for that one reading, which is still a run since the previous evaluation; a second evaluation on one day makes the noted date today, so on a first reading the window is empty and a needless rerun is named, which fails loud. Neither is ordinary, neither is changed; check: an evaluation's Dependency scan section contradicted by the Actions tab. Its confirming read, Consider: a run queued at one reading that completes later, with the next Sunday's schedule missing, reads green on a run a week old, a real and unread scan since the previous evaluation, on either branch, clearing the week after, so the rule stands; evaluate-reports.md's "its `last run read:` time" in the sentence on step 4's file may read "value" at its next edit.

One `rm` on an ordinary layout: BUG-041 takes it before release T's stamp, a missing snapshot with its hash copy standing going to the rewritten return. ISS-026.
- **Convergent, outside the five, recorded (Review A and the audit, round 3):** the exit-line search falls back to an earlier line on `-9`, an eleven-digit code or `0x1`, `-9` being what the launcher writes on its POSIX path; on Windows the launcher never writes one and the `.err` is reviewer-writable anyway. A void that cannot be written leaves the launch admissible at the next verify, only the printed line holding it, as Requirement 2 of the round 3 brief commissions.

A skip-worktree change survives the restore, `git status` empty, the merged tree unaffected (audit A4). A planted record for an ID with no record yet is trusted on first read and passes the ceiling (audit A5, beside the branch rule's unprotected records). A record and its hash copy both deleted gives a mark, and a record changed with its copy deleted is taken as it stands, both acts on a copy outside the checkout (D-003's prose). A `write_rounds` OSError at a flag's return turns exit 1 into exit 2, failing closed. A reply already counted stays beside a later flag, failing closed, no mark. A record tampered then restored byte for byte loses the void, the line having been printed (Review A's S4, round 2, ruled a stated limit). A huge snapshot's MemoryError. The record read twice in the rewritten path (Consider).

### BUG-041: a snapshot deleted after it was taken (2026-11-02)
- **One builder round.** Review A (fix mode) APPROVE, the security audit PASS with findings, none blocking; the launcher's count 1, the guard's 0 until Review B. ISS-026.
- **Stated limits of the fix (docs/debug/BUG-041.md):** the snapshot and its hash copy both deleted reads as never taken; a reviewer running the guard's own `snapshot` rewrites both; after the flag the next `snapshot` on the same tree takes what was left as its baseline, the printed line being what stops the supervisor, and the flagged launch stays voided; a stale hash copy (a repository re-created at the same path, or a cleared `.git/bridge`) reads as GATE-INTEGRITY, and for a name with a rounds record `snapshot` is refused, exit 125, until the rounds hash copy is removed by hand: it fails closed.
- **Convergent, outside the five, recorded (Review A and the audit), graded by R-050 as main holds it, by hand ahead of release T's install (INVENTORY, Decisions):** the snapshot restored byte for byte between `load_snapshot` and `snapshot_rewritten` gives `verify` an uncaught `KeyError 'git'` on the empty dict, a traceback, exit 1, no void, the mark already removed, and `premerge` over a hand-written mark with no `snapshot` key a `KeyError 'head'`, no record (H1, A3; the brief's packager named the risk first): it needs a process hitting that window, and the shape `load_snapshot` saw carried into the verdict is the fix. A hash copy that is not UTF-8, the snapshot deleted, gives a `UnicodeDecodeError` traceback, exit 1, no flag, no void, where it was CANNOT VERIFY before; the read pre-exists and crashes beside a standing snapshot too; it takes a write outside the checkout (H2, A5). `snapshot_rewritten`'s docstring still describes `'rewritten'` as a hash difference alone, and the module docstring's exit lines do not name the deletion (D1, A5, the diagram specialist), the next increment that edits the file fixing them (REFAC-004 was that increment and, as a pure pass, left them: BUG-042 fixes them). D-003's new self-loop on `no_record` (a hash copy standing with no rounds record) has no test yet: TASK-044's per-transition list names it.
- **A security lead, outside the guard's threat model (INVENTORY, Decisions; the audit's A1, deliberate, pre-existing): the hash key follows a path the reviewer controls.** A hook planted, then `.git` moved under the checkout with a gitfile in its place, or the snapshot deleted with a planted `.git/commondir` naming a copy: `verify` exits 2, no flag, no void, and `snapshot` exits 0 with the hook in place. It is BUG-041's symptom by another route, not by anything done in passing, and neither step happens on an ordinary layout. For a program release: the common dir's identity recorded outside the checkout, or a gitfile or `commondir` the snapshot did not record refused.
- **Recorded, deliberate (the audit's A2):** a snapshot made unreadable (`icacls ... /deny`) is CANNOT VERIFY, not a flag; the next `snapshot` is refused too until the ACL clears, so nothing is laundered. The fix's shape, were it wanted: an `OSError` on that read takes the rewritten route where the copy stands.
- **Noted for the operator:** the summary lines of `verify` and `premerge` say "rewritten" for a deletion, the line above them saying "deleted", as the brief required. Symlinks and a case-sensitive file system were not run (Windows refused the links); by reading they take the moved-aside route.
- **Review B's note on TASK-043's round, recorded here because that head could not change:** `launch_info`'s started-line pattern collapses a launcher id with a same-second `Z-<n>` suffix onto that second, so a second launch in that second stays voided, refusing the merge: it fails closed.
- **The supervisor's slips, each caught and fixed before Review B:** a test asserted the word "deleted" against output whose temporary paths hold it, so mutant m04 would have survived (Review A); the record's recovery sentence was untrue for a name with a rounds record (both reviewers); a commit mixed the test fix with the bug record, which scope-check read as drift, undone unpushed and split.

### Correction to project end (2026-11-04, PR #135)
- **What was wrong.** The bullet on the field acceptance above, and the status file as PR #134 merged it, said the TDP had not calibrated. It had: the TDP's commit 23bb7e5, 2026-11-04T07:08:52Z on its branch task-172, whose status file reads `Calibrated: 2026-11-04 from 2026.10.30d to 2026.11.03a, loop paused by the owner, position unchanged`, with install 148 OK and conformance 24 OK, 3 MISSING, 5 NOTE (its status file names Socket's `if:` and the spending limits, both pending there before this release, and gives the third only as calibration). That commit is before this project's records first stated the claim (db5ccb9, 07:23:15Z; reworded in c120148, 07:57:12Z; both on PR #134's head) and two hours before PR #134's merge (09:10:19Z). The TDP's main reads 2026.10.30d until its branch merges.
- **How it happened.** The supervisor read the TDP's status file before that commit and did not read it again when it wrote the records; its own sequence placed the next read after the merge, where it found this. The round's SCOPE file gave the reviewers the false claim as a stated fact, and neither was asked to read the other project (Issues ISS-024 (m)).
- **What stands now.** R-056's "and calibrated" is met: the TDP paused at a merge boundary, after its TASK-171b, and calibrated. The field acceptance stays open. The CHANGELOG entry asks three things of the TDP: the weekly workflow written and its `workflow_dispatch` run read green; its first stripped suite running with no prompt and no refusal, or the variable named; no gate reopened. It lets the acceptance be recorded at the next increment, a probe standing in until then. At the TDP's TASK-172 the second and the third are read. The first cannot be, as the next bullet says, so the acceptance is not complete at TASK-172: the weekly clause stays open until the file is written on the TDP after the release that carries the newer pin and its dispatch run is read green, or until a later entry rules on it by name. Least confident item 1's check above is unchanged. The TDP's own stand-in for the guard, run in scratch repositories, read 12 of 12 (its status file).
- **The weekly scan is not on the TDP.** The frozen block's checkout is v5; the TDP's gate has v7.0.1; the TDP's log adds that the block downloads OSV-Scanner's binary where its floor runs the action with `--no-resolve`. The configuration text names this case, "a floor pin newer than the block's waits for the bridge release that carries the newer pin", and the TDP stopped there, as that text says. The text excuses the file's wait; it does not rewrite the entry's acceptance. So the TDP has no weekly scan until a release carries the newer pin and the TDP's own increment brings its OSV-Scanner action, v2.5.1, to the block's v2.7.0 (its INVENTORY, Decisions); until then a flaw disclosed later in an unchanged dependency shows there only at its next pull request's scan (Feedback FB-084, proposal PT10).

## Release B2's design records (2026-11-04, PR #136)

`tests/test_release_t_stamp.py`'s `test_r056_the_completion_check_passes_in_done_mode` required `completion-check.py --mode done` to read RESULT OK at all times, so it failed once R-057 to R-064 stood planned: the full suite's one failure with this pull request's variables (`1 failed, 3560 passed, 40 skipped`). TASK-045's Done when asks for the stamp test and that check to be green at the accept commit; the test made that one moment permanent, so the first plan with new rows was bound to fail it. The test now excuses a later release's rows still planned, only at `Phase: changing` and only as `NOT BUILT` lines for rows after R-056; at `Phase: done` the check must read RESULT OK as before, and any other problem still fails. `test_r056_only_a_change_cycle_s_later_planned_rows_are_excused` pins those limits. A test narrowed with its reason stated, not a gate weakened: both reviewers of the pull request are told.

- **Rounds.** Three builder rounds (composer-2.5, native profile), the launcher's count 3, the guard's 0 before Review B: round 1's reader read refused characters as whitespace and hid a key behind a desync between two scanners; round 2's Review A and the security audit converged on the same class, so the round-3 fix brief required one per-line lexer and one character rule per line and listed the dead code (ISS-029). ### TASK-047: the status file's header in its six readers (2026-11-07, PR #138)
- **Rounds.** Four builder rounds (composer-2.5, native profile), the launcher's count 4, the guard's 1 before Review B's second round. Round 1: Review A REJECT (an unterminated header raised out of the fallback; the `Workflows:` grammar in two copies; the hook dropping the traits wording; the import without a sink or the bytecode setting; the phase shown read from a malformed header) and the security audit FAIL on the first (the loop guard allowed a stop that f68a883 refused). Round 2 ended at the launcher's limit with its edits done (ISS-030), the work kept; Review A REJECT on one blocking finding, a regression on an ordinary typo (a part named twice: the shared reader returned at the duplicate, dropping the line's other problem, and workflow-check crashed, disagreed or gave a false trait finding), the audit PASS. Round 3, at the review ceiling, carried that finding alone: Review A APPROVE, the audit PASS, Review B APPROVE in its first round. Then CI on PR #138 failed one test on ubuntu-latest that Windows here always skips (no symbolic-link privilege): run through a link, conformance-check loaded `bridge_env.py` from the link's folder and fell back to its stand-in, so the Workflows-line grade, which since round 1 calls `bridge_env.status_workflow_line`, ended `not checked` (a regression: main took the grammar from workflow-check, loaded beside the real file). Round 4, above the ceiling, lifted by the class line `Blocking class: failing test - CI on PR #138, bridge programs (ubuntu-latest), run 42359390308: tests/test_workflows_line_check.py::test_r015_checker_is_found_beside_the_real_file_not_the_link[conformance-check.py]`, changed one line (`_load_bridge_env` reads `os.path.realpath(__file__)`'s folder): Review A CONFIRMED the class line against d7d9c3c and APPROVED, the audit PASS WITH FINDINGS (none blocking).
- **Round 2's findings outside the five on an ordinary layout, not fixed:** (S1) `_status_body_lines` keeps the CR of a CRLF line, so `status_workflows` on a raw CRLF string refuses `Workflows: not yet classified`; every reader opens the file with universal newlines, so none reaches it. (S2) session-start prints a refusal that is a `str` and one of the four wordings, without the brief's printable-ASCII test; with the real module the four are ASCII. (S3) without a usable `bridge_env.py`, conformance-check's `project.json` phase comparison goes quiet with no row naming it, and its MISSING row says `(ImportError)` whatever the error. (S5) conformance-check's `_escape_field` and inventory-check's `escape_field` write a tab, CR and LF as `\t`, `\r`, `\n`, where workflow-check's writes `\x09`, `\x0d`, `\x0a`; the three copies are one helper's work. (C1) the checkers test `bridge_env` for `status_values` only, so a mixed install holding it without `status_workflow_line` or `STATUS_WORKFLOWS_PROBLEMS` ends workflow-check with a traceback; (C3) conformance-check then blames `workflow-check.py`. (C2) loop-guard's import has no stdout sink, so a `bridge_env` printing at import writes into the Stop hook's reply, and it names the error by `type(err).__name__`, not the hook's safe name. `_safe_exc_type_name` and `_bridge_env_refuse_line` are copied between workflow-check and inventory-check. The round-2 report restated its counts in prose and named one of five full-tree failures (the supervisor's full run: 4567 passed, none failed). Accepted, unrequested: `dont_write_bytecode` around conformance-check's load by file.
- **The audit's advisories (round 2):** (1) `_status_prose_body` takes a later rule line as the closer whatever lies between, so a header-less file whose first line is a Markdown rule `---` and whose Phase line sits above a later `---` reads phase `""`, and the loop guard allows a stop that a7b52fd and f68a883 refused; no status file of the four projects holds a `---` line. Its fix: a closer counts only after header-shaped lines. An unterminated header's `phase: done` is read by the prose's case-insensitive pattern (all three versions alike). (4) A partial install (a new loop-guard beside an old `bridge_env` without `status_values`) leaves the guard off on every stop, one stderr line the model does not see: Requirement 10's decided fail-open.
- **Round 1's, kept by decision:** a `bridge_env` package or `.pyd` beside a program loads before the file (session-start keeps its plain import, D-002's solid edge, rather than a load by file, the dotted edge D-002 does not draw for it); session-start shows `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` in its malformed line, not the path it read. loop-guard keeps `read_status`, which `tests/test_loop_guard.py` calls, though Requirement 10 asked its removal.

### TASK-048: the plan's and the briefs' headers in their five readers (2026-11-08, PR #139)
- **Rounds.** Three builder rounds (composer-2.5, native profile), the review ceiling reached at round 3. Round 1 (46c7dc0): the security audit FAIL (B1: scope-check enforced the whole text's Scope, header block included, where the agreement read the body's, so a `## Scope` comment as the header's last line made a drifting commit read clean; B2: a brief with two front-matter blocks and no prose `Blocking class:` line read as a class lift with an empty line, unlimited rounds above the ceiling) and Review A REJECT; the cause common to most findings: header handling in each program (its own `record:` pre-filter, its own choice of whole text or body, swallowed errors) and three plan walkers. Round 2 (7dacef3): the audit FAIL (the brief comparison inverted, run only for a disagreeing header; an increment heading before the first stage of a headed plan) and Review A REJECT (four plan walkers; the merged Scope with an unbalanced parenthesis given a sentinel that could never agree, 15 of this repository's increments; six brief functions). Round 3 (62c3f6d): one brief reading (`read_brief_record`), one plan walker (`walk_plan_prose_body`), one text reading (`read_record_text`); the audit PASS with advisories; Review A REJECT on one finding, B-1 below.
- **The split verdict, ruled on the merits (the tail rule):** B-1, in a plan with no header an increment with two `Satisfies:` lines keeps only the last in completion-check's planned set, so the first line's R-ids read unplanned. Not blocking: the planning template gives one `Satisfies:` line per increment and this repository's plan has 54 for 54 increments; plan-check's parse always kept the last value; it fails closed (an R-id reported unplanned, never one let through); the header-less path is transitional (TASK-051 and TASK-054 give every plan its header). The audit graded it informational. Its fix, Review A's: the walker keeps every field line in order and completion-check unions the `Satisfies:` lines.
- **Review A's round-3 leads, not fixed:** (H-1) the orphan heading's SHAPE line is printed twice; (H-2) dead whole-text fallbacks remain beside the body readings; (H-3) R3-T2's residue: `plan_body`'s no-op raise, an `except HeaderError: pass` in bridge_env, the dead `_SCOPE_PAREN` and `ScopeAgreementError`'s docstring, though the round-3 report says they were removed; (H-4) the launcher's plan reading runs `STAGE_RX` where plan-check tests `startswith("## ")`; (H-5) `declared_set_for_id` opens with a plain `open` and reads the SCOPE files through `merge_unit_ids`; (H-6) the refusal prefix `bridge-run: refused - ` is spelled in bridge_env, and private helpers are called across modules.
- **The audit's round-3 advisories:** (A1) as H-5; (A2) `STAGE_RX` now runs in the launcher (as H-4); (A3) the dead swallow paths (as H-2); (A4) a `PlanFileReadRefusal` line already carries `bridge-run: refused - `, a doubled prefix where a caller adds it again; (A5) the launcher reads every plan file twice, once for `plan_header_refusal` and again in `plan_increment_ids`, which refuses the same unreadable file and malformed header itself (the mutation sweep's r17 is equivalent for this reason). Informational: `Satisfies:` keeps the last value (B-1).
